A Computation of the Tamarkin–Tsygan Calculus
Jun Chen, Xiabing Ruan, Jia Yang

Abstract
We compute the full Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus of a Koszul algebra whose global dimension exceeds the number of generators. Our results show that even for algebras possessing an economic presentation and agreeable homological properties, the Hochschild (co)homology, as well as the structure of the Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus may exhibit a rather intricate behavior.



1
Introduction

The protagonist of this article is the algebra
A=\mathbf{k}\langle x,y,z\rangle/(x^{2}+yx,xz,zy)
, introduced by Iyudu and Shkarin in
[
12
]
as a counterexample to the conjecture in the textbook
[
13
]
of Polishchuk and Positselski:

Conjecture 1.1

(
[
13
]
, Section 7)
.

A Koszul algebra with finite global dimension
n
has at least
n
generators.

It has been proved in
[
12
]
that
A
is Koszul and has global dimension
4
. Clearly,
A
has
3
generators. Hence,
A
serves as a counterexample to Conjecture
1.1
.

In commutative algebra, the Auslander–Buchsbaum–Serre theorem (
[
3
,
15
]
) states that for Noetherian local rings, regularity is equivalent to having finite global dimension, and in such a case, the global dimension equals to the dimension of the tangent space at the point corresponding to the maximal ideal. While the algebra
A
is not a local ring, its completion
\hat{A}=\mathbf{k}\langle\!\langle x,y,z\rangle\!\rangle/(x^{2}+yx,xz,zy)
is, with the same number of generators and the same global dimension as
A
. If the non-commutative analogue of Auslander–Buchsbaum–Serre theorem were true, then
\hat{A}
should be “regular” in a good sense. However, the fact that the global dimension exceeds the number of generators suggests that such non-commutative analogue is unlikely to hold. This points toward a kind of singularity that the algebra
A
may possess in the non-commutative setting, if one attempts to use naive generalizations of various notions from commutative algebra. For us, this algebra serves as an interesting nontrivial example for which we compute the structure known as the Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus.

The notion of Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus was originally introduced by Tamarkin and Tsygan in
[
17
]
. It encodes a rich compatible structure on the Hochschild (co)homology of an associative algebra. The Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus of
A
consists of the following data (by convention, the Hochschild cohomology is concentrated in non-positive degrees):

•

the Hochschild homology
\HH_{*}(A)
and Hochschild cohomology
\HH^{*}(A)
;

•

the cup product
\cup:\HH^{*}(A)\otimes\HH^{*}(A)\to\HH^{*}(A)
of degree
0
;

•

the cap product
\cap:\HH^{*}(A)\otimes\HH_{*}(A)\to\HH_{*}
of degree
0
;

•

the Gerstenhaber bracket
[-,-]:\HH^{*}(A)\otimes\HH^{*}(A)\to\HH^{*}(A)
of degree
1
;

•

the Connes’ differential
B:\HH_{*}(A)\to\HH_{*}(A)
of degree 1;

with appropriate compatibility conditions.

In the commutative case, the Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg isomorphism in
[
11
]
states that, for a commutative algebra
C
over a field of characteristic zero,

•

The Hochschild homology
\HH_{*}(C)
is isomorphic to the Kähler differential forms of
C
;

•

the Hochschild cohomology
\HH^{*}(C)
is isomorphic to the poly vector fields of
C
.

Later, Calaque proved in
[
5
,
6
]
that

•

The cup product of
\HH^{*}(C)
is the wedge product of poly vector fields;

•

the cap product is the contraction of differential forms by multi-vector fields, i.e. the interior product.

It was also discovered and proved by Rinehart and Connes in
[
8
,
14
]
that

•

The Connes’ differential on
\HH_{*}(C)
is the de Rham differential.

While certain developments in non-commutative algebra and geometry exist, a precise geometric meaning of the Hochschild (co)homology and the Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus in the non-commutative case remains elusive. Nevertheless, it has been proved by Armenta, Keller and Tamaroff in
[
1
,
2
,
19
]
that the Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus is a derived invariant. In this article, we compute these structures of the algebra
A
mentioned above, hoping to give some guidance for a better understanding of the possible geometry in non-commutative case. The upshot of our computation can be summarized as follows. The cap products and Gerstenhaber brackets are rather complicated with no apparent pattern to concise description. In contrast, the cup products and the Connes’ differentials are more tractable: All the cup products are either identity or zero, and the Connes’ differentials admit simple compact formulas.

The article is organized as follows. Section
2
sets up basic facts about the algebra
A
, including the Koszul resolution as the minimal free resolution, a
\mathbf{k}
-basis and some algebraic properties. This section is used extensively throughout the paper. Section
3
calculates the Hochschild homology of
A
by giving explicit vector space bases of homology groups. Section
4
calculates the Hochschild cohomology of
A
, again with explicit bases. Section
5
proves that the nontrivial cup products of the Hochschild cohomology of
A
vanish. Section
6
gives explicit formulas for the cap products. Section
7
constructs homotopy inverses between two bimodule resolutions of
A
, which is used in the last two sections. Section
8
presents a complete formula for the Connes’ differential. Section
9
explicitly computes the Gerstenhaber brackets, which contains the most heavy and technical part of the computation. Appendix
A
recalls basic definitions and properties of the Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus. Appendix
B
consists of technical statements of certain lemmas, which are necessary but not central for the main parts of the paper.

Throughout this paper,
\mathbf{k}
is a field of characteristic
0
. All the unadorned tensor products and
\Hom
spaces are over
\mathbf{k}
. We always denote by
A
the algebra
\mathbf{k}\langle x,y,z\rangle/(x^{2}+yx,xz,zy)
, and
A^{e}:=A\otimes A^{\mathrm{op}}
the enveloping algebra of
A
. The Hochschild homology is defined to be
\HH_{*}(A):=\Tor_{*}^{A^{e}}(A,A)
, and the Hochschild cohomology is defined to be
\HH^{*}(A):=\Ext^{-*}_{A^{e}}(A,A)
, with the latter placed in non-positive degrees.

2
Preliminaries

In this section, we collect some basic facts about the algebra
A
, including a minimal free resolution (the Koszul resolution) and a
\mathbf{k}
-basis.

It is shown in
[
9
,
12
]
that the algebra
A
is Koszul. Hence the minimal resolutions of
\mathbf{k}
over
A
, and of
A
over
A^{e}
are given by the Koszul resolution:

Proposition 2.1
.

The weight components of the Koszul dual coalgebra
A^{{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}}
of
A
are given by:

\begin{array}[]{l}V_{0}:=A^{{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}}_{0}=\mathbf{k},\\
V_{1}:=A^{{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}}_{1}=\mathbf{k}\{x,y,z\},\\
V_{2}:=A^{{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}}_{2}=\mathbf{k}\{(x+y)x,xz,zy\},\\
V_{3}:=A^{{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}}_{3}=\mathbf{k}\{(x+y)xz,xzy\},\\
V_{4}:=A^{{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}}_{4}=\mathbf{k}\{(x+y)xzy\},\\
V_{n}:=A^{{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}}_{n}=0\quad\text{for}\quad n\geqslant 5.\end{array}

whence the
Koszul resolution
given by
K_{n}:=A\otimes V_{n}\otimes A
with differential

\begin{array}[]{l}d_{4}:K_{4}\to K_{3},\quad 1\otimes((x+y)xzy)\otimes 1\mapsto(x+y)\otimes xzy\otimes 1-1\otimes((x+y)xz)\otimes y\\
d_{3}:K_{3}\to K_{2},\quad\begin{cases}1\otimes((x+y)xz)\otimes 1\mapsto(x+y)\otimes xz\otimes 1-1\otimes((x+y)x)\otimes z,\\
\ 1\otimes(xzy)\otimes 1\mapsto x\otimes(zy)\otimes 1-1\otimes(xz)\otimes y\end{cases}\\
d_{2}:K_{2}\to K_{1},\quad\begin{cases}1\otimes\left(x^{2}+yx\right)\otimes 1\mapsto(x+y)\otimes x\otimes 1+1\otimes x\otimes x+1\otimes y\otimes x,\\
\ 1\otimes(xz)\otimes 1\mapsto x\otimes z\otimes 1+1\otimes x\otimes z,\ 1\otimes(zy)\otimes 1\mapsto z\otimes y\otimes 1+1\otimes z\otimes y\end{cases}\\
d_{1}:K_{1}\to K_{0},\quad\begin{cases}1\otimes x\otimes 1\mapsto x\otimes 1-1\otimes x,\\
1\otimes y\otimes 1\mapsto y\otimes 1-1\otimes y\\
1\otimes z\otimes 1\mapsto z\otimes 1-1\otimes z\end{cases}\end{array}

and the augmentation
\mu:K_{0}=A\otimes A\to A
given by the multiplication of
A
, is the minimal free resolution of
A
over
A^{e}
, or the two-sided minimal free resolution of
A
.

Proof.

By definition,
V_{n}=A^{{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}}_{n}=\cap_{i+j+2=n}V^{\otimes i}\otimes R\otimes V^{\otimes j}
where
V=\mathbf{k}\{x,y,z\}
and
R
is the ideal
(x^{2}+yx,xz,zy)
of relations of
A
. Then a basis of
V_{n}
is as claimed by direct inspection. It is also straightforward to check that the maps
d_{n}
’s are indeed differentials. That the associated Koszul resolution is a minimal resolution of the
A
is a classical result, following from the Koszulness of
A
.
∎

In this article, let
V_{\bullet}
always denote
A^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm!`}}_{n}
, the weight components of the Koszul dual coalgebra of
A
, whose bases are given as in Proposition
2.1
.

Next, we use Gröbner bases to give a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
A
consisting of monomials.

Proposition 2.2

(
[
9
]
)
.

Consider the graded lexicographic order
<
on the set of monomials of
\mathbf{k}\langle x,y,z\rangle
corresponding to the order on the generators
z<y<x.

The Gröbner basis of
A
under this order is
\mathcal{G}=\{xy^{n}x+y^{n+1}x,xz,zy\}_{n\geqslant 0}
, with the leading monomials

\mathrm{Tip}(\mathcal{G})=\{xy^{n}x,xz,zy\}_{n\geqslant 0}.

Corollary 2.3
.

The algebra
A
admits a
\mathbf{k}
-basis consisting of the monomials of the following types:

Type 0:

1;

Type 1:

y^{n_{0}};

Type 2:

y^{n_{0}}x;

Type 3:

y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}};

Type 4:

y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}};

Type 5:

y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x;

Type 6:

y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}};

Type 7:

x;

Type 8:

xy^{n_{1}};

Type 9:

xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}};

Type 10:

xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x;

Type 11:

xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}};

Type 12:

y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}};

Type 13:

y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x;

Type 14:

y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}};

Type 15:

z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}};

Type 16:

z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x;

Type 17:

z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},

where
p\geqslant 1,
all exponents
n_{0},n_{1},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}
are positive integers greater than or equal to
1,
and
and the notation
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}
denotes the alternating product
z^{m_{1}}xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p-1}}xy^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}},

in which powers of
z
,
x
, and powers of
y
are multiplied sequentially.
We denote this basis by
\mathcal{B}
, and denote
\mathcal{B}_{+}=\mathcal{B}\setminus\{1\}.

Convention.
Throughout this paper, for given positive integers
n_{2},...,n_{p},m_{1},...,m_{p},
whenever
i\leqslant j,
we use the notation

z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{j}}=z^{m_{i}}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{j-1}}xy^{n_{j}}z^{m_{j}}.

The following remark will be used many times in the following sections.

Remark 2.4
.

For the sake of convenience, we denote every finite sequence
(a_{i},a_{i+1}\cdots,a_{j})
by
a_{i,\cdots,j}
for
i\leqslant j.

By Corollary
2.3
, an element
a\in A
(of degree
r
) can be written as

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}1+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+a_{7}x+\sum a_{8}(n_{1})xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum a_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{5}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\end{array}

where all the letters
n_{0},n_{1},\cdots,n_{p},n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}
and
p
are positive integers, and all the sums are taken over the possible values of those letters such that the monomials are of degree
r
. The
a_{i}
’s are coefficients in
\mathbf{k}
, depending on the powers
n_{0},n_{1},\cdots,n_{p},n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}
.

When we do not use the dependence of
a_{i}
on the letters
n_{j},m_{k}
, we may also write as

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}1+\sum a_{1}y^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{2}y^{n_{0}}x+\sum a_{3}y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+a_{7}x+\sum a_{8}xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum a_{4}y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{5}y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{6}y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{9}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{10}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{11}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{12}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{13}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{14}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{15}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{16}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{17}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\end{array}

for simplicity. We will always express the elements of
A
using the basis
\mathcal{B}
.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
a\in A
, then we have the following implications:

i

a(x+y)=0

\implies

a=0
;

ii

ya=0

\implies

a=0
;

iii

ax=0

\implies

a=a^{\prime}(x+y)
for some
a^{\prime}\in A
;

iv

xa=0

\implies

a=za^{\prime}
for some
a^{\prime}\in A
;

Proof.

Since
A
is Koszul, its left Koszul resolution

K^{L}_{\bullet}:=A\otimes V_{\bullet}
with the differentials

\begin{array}[]{l}d^{L}_{4}:K^{L}_{4}\to K^{L}_{3},\quad a\otimes((x+y)xzy)\mapsto a(x+y)\otimes xzy,\\
d^{L}_{3}:K^{L}_{3}\to K^{L}_{2},\quad\begin{cases}a\otimes((x+y)xz)\mapsto a(x+y)\otimes xz,\\
b\otimes(xzy)\mapsto bx\otimes(zy),\end{cases}\\
d^{L}_{2}:K^{L}_{2}\to K^{L}_{1},\quad\begin{cases}a\otimes\left(x^{2}+yx\right)\mapsto a(x+y)\otimes x,\\
b\otimes(xz)\mapsto bx\otimes z,\\
c\otimes(zy)\mapsto cz\otimes y,\end{cases}\\
d^{L}_{1}:K^{L}_{1}\to K^{L}_{0},\quad a\otimes p\mapsto ap,\ p=x,y,z,\end{array}

is exact except for at degree
0
.

For
a\otimes((x+y)xzy)\in K_{4}^{L}
,
a\otimes((x+y)xzy)\in\ker d_{4}^{L}
if and only if
a(x+y)=0
. By exactness,
\ker d_{4}^{L}=0
, so
a=0
. This gives (
i
).

Let
b\otimes xzy\subseteq K_{3}^{L}
, then
b\otimes xzy\in\ker d_{3}^{L}
if and only if
bx=0
. By exactness,
\ker d_{3}^{L}=\im d_{4}^{L}=\{a(x+y)\otimes xzy\mid a\in A\}
. Hence
bx=0
if and only if
b=a(x+y)
for some
a\in A
. This gives (
iii
).

Similarly, the right Koszul resolution

K_{\bullet}^{R}:=V_{\bullet}\otimes A
of
A
with differentials

\begin{array}[]{l}d_{4}^{R}:K_{4}^{R}\to K_{3}^{R},\quad((x+y)xzy)\otimes a\mapsto((x+y)xz)\otimes ya,\\
d_{3}^{R}:K_{3}^{R}\to K_{2}^{R},\quad\begin{cases}((x+y)xz)\otimes a\mapsto((x+y)x)\otimes za,\\
(xzy)\otimes b\mapsto xz\otimes yb\end{cases}\\
d_{2}^{R}:K_{2}^{R}\to K_{1}^{R},\quad\begin{cases}((x+y)x)\otimes a\mapsto x\otimes xa+y\otimes xa,\\
xz\otimes b\mapsto x\otimes zb,\end{cases}\\
d_{1}^{R}:K_{1}^{R}\to K_{0}^{R},\quad p\otimes a\mapsto pa,p=x,y,z,\end{array}

is exact except for at degree
0
.

For
((x+y)xzy)\otimes a\in K_{4}^{R}
,
((x+y)xzy)\otimes a\in\ker d_{4}^{R}
if and only if
ya=0
. By exactness,
\ker d_{4}^{R}=0
, so
a=0
. This gives (
ii
).

Let
((x+y)x)\otimes a\in K_{3}^{R}
, then
((x+y)x)\otimes a\in\ker d_{2}^{R}
if and only if
xa=0
. By exactness,
\ker d_{2}^{R}=\im d_{3}^{R}=\{((x+y)x)\otimes zb+(xzy)\otimes yc\mid b,c\in A\}
. Observe that
((x+y)x)\otimes a\in\im d_{3}^{R}
if and only if
a=zb
for some
b\in A
. This gives (
iv
).
∎

3
Hochschild Homology

In this section, we compute explicitly the Hochschild homology of
A
. We will give a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
\HH_{d}(A)
at each degree
d\geqslant 0
using the
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
A
computed in Corollary
2.3
.

According to Definition
A.3
,
\HH_{\bullet}(A)
is isomorphic to the homology of the chain complex
K_{\bullet}\otimes_{A^{e}}A
. Under the isomorphisms
A\otimes_{A^{e}}K_{\bullet}=A\otimes_{A^{e}}(A\otimes V_{\bullet}\otimes A)\cong A\otimes V_{\bullet}
, it suffices to compute the homology of the following complex:

0\to A\otimes V_{4}\xrightarrow{d_{4}}A\otimes V_{3}\xrightarrow{d_{3}}A\otimes V_{2}\xrightarrow{d_{2}}A\otimes V_{1}\xrightarrow{d_{1}}A\to 0,

where the boundary maps
d_{i}=\id_{A}\otimes d_{i}
are induced from
(K_{\bullet},d_{\bullet})
and still denoted by
d_{i}
by abusing the notation. More precisely, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}d_{1}:A\otimes V_{1}\rightarrow A,\quad a\otimes p\mapsto ap-pa,p=x,y,z.\\
d_{2}:A\otimes V_{2}\rightarrow A\otimes V_{1},\quad\begin{cases}a\otimes\left(x^{2}+yx\right)\mapsto a(x+y)\otimes x+xa\otimes x+xa\otimes y)\\
b\otimes(xz)\mapsto bx\otimes z+zb\otimes x\\
c\otimes(zy)\mapsto cz\otimes y+yc\otimes z\end{cases},\\
d_{3}:A\otimes V_{3}\rightarrow A\otimes V_{2},\quad\begin{cases}a\otimes xzy\mapsto ax\otimes zy-ya\otimes xz\\
b\otimes\left(x^{2}z+yxz\right)\mapsto b(x+y)\otimes xz-zb\otimes\left(x^{2}+yx\right)\end{cases},\\
d_{4}:A\otimes V_{4}\rightarrow A\otimes V_{3},\quad a\otimes\left(x^{2}zy+yxzy\right)\mapsto a(x+y)\otimes xzy+ya\otimes\left(x^{2}z+yxz\right).\end{array}

By Definition,
\HH_{i}(A)=\ker d_{i}/\im d_{i+1}
. Now let us compute them degree by degree. Since
V_{n}=0
for
n\geqslant 5
, the homologies of degree greater than
5
vanish automatically. It suffices to compute on degree
0,1,2,3,4
.

Proposition 3.1
.

The Hochschild homology of
A
vanishes in degree
4
.

Proof.

Since
d_{5}=0
, we have
\HH_{4}(A)=\ker d_{4}
. For
a\otimes(x^{2}zy+yxzy)\in A\otimes V_{4}
, we have
d_{4}(a)=a(x+y)\otimes xzy+ya\otimes(x^{2}z+yxz)
. Then
a\in\ker d_{4}
is equivalent to the conditions

\displaystyle a(x+y)=0,

(1)

\displaystyle ya=0.

(2)

By Lemma
2.5
, either (
1
) or (
2
) implies that
a=0
. Therefore,
\HH_{4}(A)=0
.
∎

Proposition 3.2
.

The Hochschild homology of
A
vanishes in degree
3
.

Proof.

Let
\alpha=a\otimes xzy+b\otimes(x^{2}z+yxz)\in\ker d_{3}
. Then
d_{3}(\alpha)=0
, whence

\displaystyle ax=0

(3)

\displaystyle b(x+y)-ya=0

(4)

\displaystyle zb=0

(5)

By Lemma
2.5
, (
3
) implies
a=a^{\prime}(x+y)
for some
a^{\prime}\in A
. Then (
4
) is

b(x+y)-ya=b(x+y)-ya^{\prime}(x+y)=(b-ya^{\prime})(x+y)=0

By Lemma
2.5
again,
b-ya^{\prime}=0
, i.e.,
b=ya^{\prime}
. So we have

\ker d_{3}\subseteq\{a^{\prime}(x+y)\otimes xzy+ya^{\prime}\otimes(x^{2}z+yxz)\mid a^{\prime}\in A\}.

On the other hand,

\im d_{4}=\left\{c(x+y)\otimes xzy+yc\otimes\left(x^{2}z+yxz\right)\mid c\in A\right\}.

By direct inspection,
\ker d_{3}\subseteq\im d_{4}
. Hence
\ker d_{3}=\im d_{4}
and
\HH_{3}(A)=0
.

∎

Similar arguments stop working at degree
2
. We now calculate
\HH_{2}(A)=\ker d_{2}/\im d_{3}
directly by first quotienting out
\im d_{3}
. The lemmas concerning the images of the differentials are placed in Appendix
B
.

Proposition 3.3
.

The Hochschild homology of
A
in degree
2
vanishes.

Proof.

For any
\alpha=c\otimes xz+d\otimes zy+e\otimes(x^{2}+yx)\in\ker d_{2}
with
c,d,e\in A
, by Lemma
B.1
, we may choose a representative of
c,d,e
in
\ker d_{2}/\im d_{3}
such that

\begin{array}[]{rl}c=&\sum c_{9}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum c_{15}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\end{array}

by adding suitable elements in
\im d_{3}
. Now
\alpha\in\ker d_{2}
if and only if

\displaystyle e(x+y)+zc+xe=0

(6)

\displaystyle xe+dz=0

(7)

\displaystyle cx+yd=0

(8)

Consider (
8
), by expressing
d
using the basis
\mathcal{B}
in Corollary
2.3
as indicated in Remark
2.4
, the term
yd
starts with
y
. By assumption and similar observation, the terms in
cx
start with either
x
or
z
. Hence (
8
) is equivalent to

cx=0=yd.

We have

\begin{array}[]{rl}cx=&c_{9}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+c_{15}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=0\\
\end{array}

which implies
c_{9}=c_{15}=0
, whence
c=0
. By Lemma
2.5
,
yd=0
implies
d=0
. Therefore, we have
c=0
and
d=0
.

Then Equation (
6
) and (
7
) say that
e(x+y)=0
, by Lemma
2.5
,
e=0
.

As a result, we have
\alpha=c\otimes xz+d\otimes zy+e\otimes(x^{2}+yx)\in\ker d_{2}
is zero after taking the quotient by
\im d_{3}
. Hence
\HH_{2}(A)=0
.
∎

Next we compute the degree
1
homology.

Proposition 3.4
.

\HH_{1}(A)
has a
\mathbf{k}
-basis consisting of the following elements:

(i).

\alpha_{1}=1\otimes x,

(ii).

\beta_{1}=x\otimes x,

(iii).

\gamma_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}x\otimes x,\quad n_{0}\geq 1,

(iv).

\delta_{1}=1\otimes y,

(v).

\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}\otimes y,\quad n_{0}\geq 1

(vi).

\zeta_{1}=1\otimes z,

(vii).

\eta_{1}(n_{1})=z^{n_{1}}\otimes z,\quad n_{1}\geq 1,

(viii).

\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=(\sum_{i=1}^{p}y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}})\otimes x

\hskip 28.45274pt+(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\otimes y

\hskip 28.45274pt+(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{s=1}^{m_{i}}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s-1})\otimes z,

where
p,n_{0},n_{1},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1
are positive integers, and we set
n_{0}=n_{p},m_{0}=m_{p}
by convention.

Note that in
\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})
, the term for
i=1
is

y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\otimes x+\sum_{t=1}^{n_{1}}y^{n_{1}-t}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{t-1}\otimes y+\sum_{s=1}^{m_{1}}z^{m_{1}-s}xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{s-1}\otimes z,

and the term for
i=p
is

y^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{n_{p-1}}\otimes x+\sum_{t=1}^{n_{p}}y^{n_{p}-t}z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{n_{p-1}}xy^{t-1}\otimes y+\sum_{s=1}^{m_{p}}z^{m_{p}-s}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p-1}}xy^{n_{p}}z^{s-1}\otimes z.

Proof.

For any
\alpha=a\otimes x+b\otimes y+c\otimes z\in\ker d_{1}/\im d_{2}
, by Lemma
B.2
and choosing suitable representatives, we may assume that

(i)

The coefficients of the terms

R_{a}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
\end{array}\right\}

in
a
are zero;

(ii)

The coefficients of the terms

R_{b}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}y^{n_{0}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
x,\\
xy^{n_{1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\end{array}\right\}

in
b
are zero;

(iii)

The coefficients of the terms

R_{c}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}y^{n_{0}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
\end{array}\right\}

in
c
are zero.

In other words, we may assume that

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}1+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+a_{7}x+\sum a_{8}(n_{1})xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum a_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{5}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rl}b=&b_{0}1+\sum b_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}\\
&+\sum b_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum b_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum b_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum b_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rl}c=&c_{0}1+\sum c_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+c_{7}x+\sum c_{8}(n_{1})xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum c_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum c_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum c_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\

&+\sum c_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
\end{array}

The condition
d_{1}(\alpha)=ax-xa+by-yb+cz-zb=0
implies that the sum of the following six expressions is zero:

\begin{array}[]{rl}ax=&a_{0}x+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x-\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}+1}x-\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}+n_{1}+1}x-a_{7}yx-\sum a_{8}(n_{1})y^{n_{1}+1}x\\

&\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rl}-xa=&-a_{0}x-\sum a_{1}(n_{0})xy^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}+1}x+\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}+a_{7}yx+\sum a_{8}(n_{1})yxy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum a_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{5}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\

&+\sum a_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})yxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\

\begin{array}[]{rl}-zc=&-c_{0}z-c_{7}zx-\sum c_{8}(n_{1})zxy^{n_{1}}\\

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}1+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+a_{7}x\\
&+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
b=&b_{0}1+\sum b_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}\\
&+\sum b_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum b_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum b_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum b_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
c=&c_{0}1\\
&+c_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+c_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+c_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+c_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
\end{array}

where the coefficients
a_{0}
,
a_{2}(n_{0})
,
a_{7}
,
b_{0}
,
b_{1}(n_{0})
and
c_{0}
are free; while the rest coefficients
a_{12}
,
b_{13}
,
b_{14}
,
b_{16}
,
b_{17}
,
c_{9}
,
c_{11}
,
c_{15}
and
c_{17}
are related to each other according to the equalities (15) – (30) above. In summary, the coefficients of the following terms in
\alpha=a\otimes x+b\otimes y+c\otimes z
are equal:

•

By (15) – (16),
(y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}})\otimes x
and
(y^{n_{1}-1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x)\otimes y
;

•

By (17) – (20),
(y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}})\otimes y
and
(y^{n_{1}-1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}+1})\otimes y
, for
n_{p+1}\geqslant 0
;

•

By (21) – (24),
(z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}})\otimes y
and
(z^{m_{1}-1}xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}+1})\otimes z
, for
n_{p+1}\geqslant 0
;

•

By (25) – (28),
(z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}})\otimes z
and
(z^{m_{1}-1}xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p-1}}xy^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}+1})\otimes z
, for
m_{p}\geqslant 0
;

•

By (29) – (30),
(xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}})\otimes z
and
(y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p-1}}xy^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}+1})\otimes x
, for
m_{p}\geqslant 0
;

where all the powers are positive integers and
p\geqslant 1
, unless otherwise specified. Now it is straightforward to verify that
\alpha_{1},\beta_{1},\gamma_{1}(n_{0}),\delta_{1},\epsilon_{1}(n_{0}),\zeta_{1},\eta_{1}(n_{1})
and
\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})
satisfy all the restrictions and are linearly independent over
\mathbf{k}
. Moreover, if we suppose all the terms of
\alpha
which appear in
\alpha_{1},\beta_{1},\gamma_{1}(n_{0}),\delta_{1},\epsilon_{1}(n_{0}),\zeta_{1},\eta_{1}(n_{1})
and
\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})
have zero coefficients, then
\alpha=0
. This completes the proof.
∎

Next we compute the
0
-th Hochschild homology.

Proposition 3.5
.

\HH_{0}(A)
has a
\mathbf{k}
-basis consisting of the following elements:

(i).

\zeta_{0}=1,

(ii).

\alpha_{0}=x,

(iii).

\beta_{0}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}x,

(iv).

\gamma_{0}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}},

(v).

\quad\delta_{0}(n_{1})=z^{n_{1}},

(vi).

\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\overline{y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x},

for
p,n_{0},n_{1},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, and the bars mean that the powers
(n_{1},m_{1}),\cdots,(n_{p},m_{p})
are determined up to a cyclic permutation, i.e.,

\overline{y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x}=\overline{y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}x}

for
i=1,\cdots,p
.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.3
, for an element
e\in\ker d_{0}=A
, we may choose a representative of
e
in
\HH_{0}(A)=A/\im d_{1}
such that the coefficients of the following terms vanish:

R=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\end{array}\right\}

Hence we may assume

\begin{array}[]{rl}e=&e_{0}+\sum e_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum e_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+e_{7}x\\
&+\sum e_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\end{array}

Note that by the relations in
\im d_{1}
, we have, in
\HH_{1}(A)
,

\begin{array}[]{l}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=y^{n_{1}-1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy=\cdots=z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}\\
=z^{m_{1}-1}xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z=\cdots=xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}=y^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}x\end{array}

whence

y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}

for all
i=2,\dots,p
. In other words,

y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=y^{n_{\sigma(1)}}z^{m_{\sigma(1)}}xy^{n_{\sigma(2)}}z^{m_{\sigma(2)}}\cdots y^{m_{\sigma(p)}}z^{m_{\sigma(p)}}x

for all permutations
\sigma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subseteq\mathbf{S}_{p}
. Hence a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
\HH_{0}(A)
consists of elements of the form as claimed.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

We know that
\HH_{0}(A)\cong A/[A,A]
, see for example
[
20
]
. So for the elements in
\HH_{0}(A)
, we can move one letter from one side to the other, without changing the homology class. The basis elements of
A
in Proposition
2.2
can be rewritten in
\HH_{0}(A)
as:

(i)

Type
1

y^{n_{0}}
is nonzero;

(ii)

Type
3
and
8
are equivalent to type
2
:
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}=y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}-1}=\cdots=y^{n_{0}+n_{1}}x
,
xy^{n_{1}}=yxy^{n_{1}-1}=\cdots=y^{n_{1}}x
;

(iii)

Type
4
is equivalent to zero:
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}=y^{n_{0}-1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}y=0
since
zy=0
;

(iv)

Type
5
is equivalent to zero:
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=y^{n_{0}-1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy=\cdots

=xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{0}}=y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{0}}x=0,

since
zxy^{n_{0}}x=-zy^{n_{0}+1}x=0;

(v)

Type
6,10
and
11
are equivalent to zero by the same reason as type
5
;

(vi)

Type
7

x
is nonzero;

(vii)

Type
9,14,15
and
17
are equivalent to type
13
:

\begin{array}[]{c}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}=y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}=y^{n_{1}+1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}-1}=\cdots y^{n_{1}+n_{p+1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}=\cdots=xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p-1}}xy^{n_{p}}z^{m_{1}+m_{p}}=y^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p-1}}xy^{n_{p}}z^{m_{1}+m_{p}}x,\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}=\cdots=y^{n_{p+1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x;\end{array}

(viii)

Type
12
is zero by the same reason as type
4
;

(ix)

Type
16
is zero:
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=z^{m_{1}-1}xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xz=0
since
xz=0
.

In conclusion, all the nonzero classes of
\HH_{0}(A)
are represented by types
1,2,7
and
13
, which agrees with Proposition
3.5
.

Corollary 3.7
.

For
n\geqslant 2
, the Hochschild homology
\HH_{n}(A)
of
A
vanishes.

4
Hochschild Cohomology

In this section, we shall compute the Hochschild cohomology
\HH^{*}(A)
of
A
.
Recall from Definition
A.3
that the Hochschild cohomology is negatively graded and
\HH^{-*}(A)
is the cohomology of the complex
\Hom_{A^{e}}(K_{\bullet},A),

where
(K_{\bullet},d_{\bullet})
is the Koszul resolution of
A
, as explicated in Proposition
2.1
.
Under the isomorphism
\Hom_{A^{e}}(A\otimes V_{\bullet}\otimes A,A)\cong\Hom_{\mathbf{k}}(V_{\bullet},A),
it suffices to compute the cohomology of the following complex:

0\to A\xrightarrow{d_{1}^{*}}\Hom(V_{1},A)\xrightarrow{d_{2}^{*}}\Hom(V_{2},A)\xrightarrow{d_{3}^{*}}\Hom(V_{3},A)\xrightarrow{d_{4}^{*}}\Hom(V_{4},A)\to 0,

(9)

where the differential
d_{i}^{*}
is given by

(i)

for
a\in A
,

\begin{array}[]{l}d_{1}^{*}(a)(x)=xa-ax,\\
d_{1}^{*}(a)(y)=ya-ay,\\
d_{1}^{*}(a)(z)=za-az;\end{array}

(ii)

for
f:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&a\\
y&\mapsto&b\\
z&\mapsto&c\end{array}\right.\in\Hom(V_{1},A),

\begin{array}[]{l}d^{*}_{2}(f)((x+y)x)=(x+y)a+ax+bx,\\
d^{*}_{2}(f)(xz)=xc+az,\\
d^{*}_{2}(f)(zy)=zb+cy;\end{array}

(iii)

for
f:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&a\\
xz&\mapsto&b\\
zy&\mapsto&c\end{array}\right.\in\Hom(V_{2},A),

\begin{array}[]{l}d^{*}_{3}(f)((x+y)xz)=(x+y)b-az,\\
d^{*}_{3}(f)(xzy)=xc-by;\end{array}

(iv)

for
f:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)xz&\mapsto&a\\
xzy&\mapsto&b\end{array}\right.\in\Hom(V_{3},A),

d^{*}_{4}(f)((x+y)xzy)=(x+y)b+ay.

Proposition 4.1
.

The degree
0
Hochschild cohomology
\HH^{0}(A)
of
A
is isomorphic to
\mathbf{k}
.

Proof.

It is obvious that
\HH^{0}(A)=\ker d_{1}^{*}
. Recall that the differential
d_{1}^{*}
takes the following form. For
a\in A
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}d_{1}^{*}(a)(x)=xa-ax,\\
d_{1}^{*}(a)(y)=ya-ay,\\
d_{1}^{*}(a)(z)=za-az.\end{array}

Let
a\in\ker(d_{1}^{*}).
Using the
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
A
in Corollary
2.3
, we assume that

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+a_{4}x+\sum a_{5}(n_{1})xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum a_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}x\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum a_{7}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{8}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}+\sum a_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum a_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}+\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}.\end{array}

The condition
d_{1}^{*}(a)=0
implies that
a
commutes with
x,y
and
z
:

\displaystyle xa-ax=0,

(10)

\displaystyle ya-ay=0,

(11)

\displaystyle za-az=0.

(12)

Equation (
10
) implies that the sum of the following two expressions is zero:

\begin{array}[]{rl}xa=&a_{0}x+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})xy^{n_{0}}-\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}+1}x-\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}-a_{4}yx-\sum a_{5}(n_{1})yxy^{n_{1}}\\
&-\sum a_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}-\sum a_{7}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&-\sum a_{8}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}-\sum a_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})yxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&-\sum a_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})yxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x-\sum a_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})yxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
-ax=&-a_{0}x-\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}+1}x+\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}+n_{1}+1}x+a_{4}yx+\sum a_{5}(n_{1})y^{n_{1}+1}x\\
&-\sum a_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x-\sum a_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&-\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x-\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x.\end{array}

Therefore,
a
reduces to the form:

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+a_{4}x+\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\end{array}

where all other coefficients vanish due to equation (
10
).

Equation (
11
) implies that the sum of the following two expressions is zero:

\begin{array}[]{rl}ya=&a_{0}y+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}+1}x+a_{4}yx+\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})yz^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})yz^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
-ay=&-a_{0}y-\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}xy-a_{4}xy-\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}+1}.\end{array}

Then the coefficients of all the terms of
a
are forced to be zero except for the constant term
a_{0}
.
In other words,
a=a_{0}
is a constant.
Then the equation (
12
)
za-az=0
holds automatically.
Hence
\HH^{0}(A)=\ker(d_{1}^{*})=\{a_{0}\mid a_{0}\in\mathbf{k}\}\cong\mathbf{k}.

∎

Proposition 4.2
.

The Hochschild cohomology
\HH^{-1}(A)
has a
\mathbf{k}
-basis consisting of the following elements:

\begin{array}[]{l}A^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}x\mapsto x\\
y\mapsto y\\
z\mapsto 0\end{array}\right),B^{-1}(n)=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&y^{n}x\\
y&\mapsto&-y^{n}x\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),C^{-1}(n+1)=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1}\\
z&\mapsto&zxy^{n}\end{array}\right),\\
D^{-1}(n,i)=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&y^{n}x-y^{i}xy^{n-i}\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\\
E^{-1}(n)=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&y^{n}x+y^{n+1}\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\\
F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\\
G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\\
H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\\
I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\\
J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
z&\mapsto&-zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}-1}\end{array}\right),\\
K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&0\\
z&\mapsto&z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\end{array}\right),\end{array}

(13)

where
n\geqslant 1,1\leqslant i\leqslant n-2,p\geqslant 1,
and
n_{i},m_{j}\geqslant 1,
for all
i
and
j.

Proof.

Recall that the differential
d_{2}^{*}
takes the following form. For
f:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&a\\
y&\mapsto&b\\
z&\mapsto&c\end{array}\right.\in\Hom(V_{1},A),
we have

\begin{array}[]{l}d^{*}_{2}(f)((x+y)x)=(x+y)a+ax+bx,\\
d^{*}_{2}(f)(xz)=xc+az,\\
d^{*}_{2}(f)(zy)=zb+cy.\end{array}

A direct computation shows that the elements
A^{-1},B^{-1}(\cdot),...,K^{-1}(\cdot)
are linearly independent and lie in
\ker d_{2}^{*}
. Let
\Phi^{-1}\subset\ker d_{2}^{*}
be the
\mathbf{k}
-vector subspace generated by these elements.

We first claim that
\Phi^{-1}\cap\im d_{1}^{*}=0.
Let
f\in\Phi^{-1}\cap\im d_{1}^{*}.

By Lemma
B.4
, a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
\im d_{1}^{*}(x)
is given by

R^{-1}_{1}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}xy^{n_{0}}-y^{n_{0}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}-y^{n_{0}+n_{1}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}-y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1.}

Furthermore, it is straigthforward to see that a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
\Phi^{-1}(x)
is given by

W^{-1}_{1}=\{x,y^{n_{0}}x\}_{n_{0}\geqslant 1}.

By observation, we have
\im d_{1}^{*}(x)\cap\Phi^{-1}(x)=0,
which implies that
f(x)=0.

Next, let
S=\{g\in\im d_{1}^{*}\mid g(x)=0\}
and
T=\{h\in\Phi^{-1}\mid h(x)=0\}
be the subspaces of
\im d_{1}^{*}
and
\Phi^{-1}
consisting of elements that annihilate
x
, respectively. By Lemma
B.4
, one can directly check that a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
S(y)
is given by

R^{-1}_{2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}y^{n_{0}}x-y^{n_{0}-1}xy,\\
yz^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x-z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy,\\
yz^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}-z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}+1}\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},n_{2},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1}

and for
T(y)
is given by

W^{-1}_{2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}y^{n_{0}+1}x-xy^{n_{0}+1},\\
y^{n_{0}}x-y^{i}xy^{n_{0}-i},\\
y^{n_{0}}x+y^{n_{0}+1},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1,1\leqslant i\leqslant n_{0}-2.}

Therefore, we have
S(y)\cap T(y)=0,
which implies
f(y)=0.

Finally, let
S^{\prime}=\{g\in\im d_{1}^{*}\mid g(x)=0,g(y)=0\}
and
T^{\prime}=\{h\in\Phi^{-1}\mid h(x)=0,h(y)=0\}
be the subspaces of
\im d_{1}^{*}
and
\Phi^{-1}
consisting of elements that annihilate
x
and
y
, respectively. One can directly check that
S^{\prime}(z)=0
and

T^{\prime}(z)=\mathbf{k}\{z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{2},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1}.

Therefore, we have
S^{\prime}(y)\cap T^{\prime}(y)=0,
which implies
f(z)=0.
Hence we have successfully checked that
\Phi^{-1}\cap\im d_{1}^{*}=0.

Since
\HH^{-1}(A)=\ker d_{2}^{*}/\im d_{1}^{*}
, the isomorphism of
\mathbf{k}
-vector spaces
\HH^{-1}(A)\cong\Phi^{-1}
holds if and only if

\ker d_{2}^{*}/(\im d_{1}^{*}\oplus\Phi^{-1})=0.

Let
f=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&a\\
y&\mapsto&b\\
z&\mapsto&c\end{array}\right)\in\ker(d_{2}^{*})

and denote
\overline{f}
be its image in the quotient space
\ker d_{2}^{*}/(\im d_{1}^{*}\oplus\Phi^{-1}).

We shall find a simple representative of
\overline{f}.

We have computed above that
\im d_{1}^{*}(x)=\mathbf{k}R^{-1}_{1}
and
\Phi^{-1}(x)=\mathbf{k}W^{-1}_{1}.

By reorganizing the
\im d_{1}^{*}(x)\oplus\Phi^{-1}(x)
admits a “leftmost term reduced” (the set of leftmost terms and the set of remainder terms are disjoint)
\mathbf{k}
-basis:

U^{-1}_{1}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}x,xy^{n_{0}},y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}},y^{n_{0}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}-y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1}.

Thus, we assume that

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}.\end{array}

Next, consider the subspace
S\oplus T
of
\im d_{1}^{*}\oplus\Phi^{-1}
that annihilates
x
.
Based on the bases
R^{-1}_{2}
of
S(y)
and
W^{-1}_{2}
of
T(y)
, it is straightforward to verify that
(S\oplus T)(y)
admits a
\mathbf{k}
-basis:

U^{-1}_{2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}xy^{n_{0}}+y^{n_{0}+1},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+y^{n_{0}+n_{1}+1},\\
y^{n_{0}}x+y^{n_{0}+1},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1}.

Therefore, we assume that

\begin{array}[]{rl}b=&b_{0}+\sum b_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+b_{7}x\\
&+\sum b_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum b_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum b_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}x\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum b_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum b_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum b_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x.\end{array}

Similarly, the subspace
S^{\prime}\oplus T^{\prime}
of
\im d_{1}^{*}\oplus\Phi^{-1}
annihilating
x
and
y
is

S^{\prime}\oplus T^{\prime}\cong\mathbf{k}\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&0\\
z&\mapsto&z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\end{array}\right)\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{2},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1.}

Thus, we assume that

\begin{array}[]{rl}c=&c_{0}+\sum c_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum c_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+\sum c_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+c_{7}x+\sum c_{8}(n_{1})xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum c_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum c_{5}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum c_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}+\sum c_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum c_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum c_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum c_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum c_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum c_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}+\sum c_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
&+\sum c_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}.\end{array}

Since
f\in\ker(d_{2}^{*}),
we have the following equations:

\displaystyle(x+y)a+ax+bx=0,

(14)

\displaystyle xc+az=0,

(15)

\displaystyle zb+cy=0.

(16)

Equation (
14
) implies that the sum of the following terms equals zero:

\begin{array}[]{rl}(x+y)a=&a_{0}x+a_{0}y+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})xy^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}+1}\\
&+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}+1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}+1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}+1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})yz^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})yz^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}};\\
ax=&a_{0}x+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x\\
&+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x;\\
bx=&b_{0}x+\sum b_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x-b_{7}yx\\
&+\sum b_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum b_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum b_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum b_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x.\end{array}

After combining like terms in the summation of the above three expressions, we obtain the following relations among the coefficients for
p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1:

(i)

a_{0}=0;

(ii)

a_{1}(n_{0})=0;

(iii)

a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=0;

(iv)

a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})=0;

(v)

a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=0;

(vi)

a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})=0;

(vii)

b_{0}=0;

(viii)

b_{1}(n_{0}+1)=0;

(ix)

b_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=0;

(x)

b_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=0;

(xi)

b_{1}(1)=b_{7};

(xii)

a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})+b_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=0;

(xiii)

a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})+b_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=0.

Hence we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
b=&b_{1}(1)y-b_{1}(1)x-\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum b_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x-\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum b_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x.\end{array}

Furthermore, Equation (
15
) implies that the sum of the following terms equals zero:

\begin{array}[]{rl}xc=&c_{0}x+\sum c_{1}(n_{0})xy^{n_{0}}-\sum c_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}+1}x-\sum c_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}-c_{7}yx-\sum c_{8}(n_{1})yxy^{n_{1}}\\
&-\sum c_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}-\sum c_{5}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&-\sum c_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}-\sum c_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})yxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&-\sum c_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})yxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x-\sum c_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})yxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum c_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+\sum c_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum c_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
az=&0.\end{array}

Consequently, all coefficients of the aforementioned terms vanish. Hence we have

c=\sum c_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum c_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}.

Finally, Equation(
16
) implies that the sum of the following terms equals zero:

\begin{array}[]{rl}zb=&-b_{1}(1)zx-\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum b_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+\sum b_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}+1}xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
cy=&\sum c_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy+\sum c_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}+1}.\end{array}

Consequently, all coefficients of the aforementioned terms vanish.
Therefore,
a,b,c
are zero and hence
f=0
, which completes the proof.
∎

The argument for
\HH^{\leqslant-2}
parallels that of
\HH^{-1}
. We omit the repetitive details for brevity

Proposition 4.3
.

The Hochschild cohomology
\HH^{-2}(A)
has a
\mathbf{k}
-basis consisting of the following elements:

\begin{array}[]{l}A^{-2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&x\\
zy&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\quad B^{-2}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
zy&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\end{array}

where
p\geqslant 1,
and
n_{i},m_{j}\geqslant 1,
for all
i
and
j.

Proof.

Recall that the differential
d_{3}^{*}
is given by: for
f:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&a\\
xz&\mapsto&b\\
zy&\mapsto&c\end{array}\right.\in\Hom(V_{2},A)
,

\begin{array}[]{l}d^{*}_{3}(f)((x+y)xz)=(x+y)b-az,\\
d^{*}_{3}(f)(xzy)=xc-by.\end{array}

A direct computation shows that the elements
A^{-2},B^{-2}(\cdot)
are linearly independent and lie in
\ker d_{3}^{*}.
Let
\Phi^{-2}\subset\ker d_{3}^{*}
be the
\mathbf{k}
-subspace generated by these elements.

Similar to the
\HH^{-1}(A)
case, we first prove that
\im d_{2}^{*}\cap\Phi^{-2}=0.
Let
f\in\im d_{2}^{*}\cap\Phi^{-2}.
By Lemma
B.5
, a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
\im d_{2}^{*}(xz)
is given by

R_{1}^{-2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}x,xy^{n_{0}},y^{n_{0}}x,y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1,}

and
\Phi^{-2}(xz)=0.

Let
S=\{f\in\im d_{2}^{*}\mid f(xz)=0\}
and
T=\{g\in\Phi^{-2}\mid g(xz)=0\}
be the subspaces of
\im d_{2}^{*}
and
\Phi^{-2}
annihilating
xz
, respectively. Then
S(zy)
admits a
\mathbf{k}
-basis given by

R_{2}^{-2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{2},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1,}

and
T(zy)=0.

Let
S^{\prime}=\{f\in\im d_{2}^{*}\mid f(xz)=0,f(zy)=0\}
and
T^{\prime}=\{g\in\Phi^{-2}\mid g(xz)=0,g(zy)=0\}
denote the subspaces of
\im d_{2}^{*}
and
\Phi^{-2}
annihilating
xz
and
zy
, respectively. Then
S^{\prime}((x+y)x)
admits a
\mathbf{k}
-basis given by

R_{3}^{-2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}y^{n_{0}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1,}

and
T^{\prime}((x+y)x)
admits a
\mathbf{k}
-basis given by

W^{-2}_{3}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}x,\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{2},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1.}

Therefore, from
\Phi^{-2}(xz)=0,T(zy)=0,
and
R_{3}^{-2}\cap W^{-2}_{3}=\emptyset,
we deduce
a=b=c=0,
which further implies
f=0.
Hence
\im d_{2}^{*}\cap\Phi^{-2}=0.

It remains to show that
\ker d_{3}^{*}/(\im d_{2}^{*}\oplus\Phi^{-2})=0.

Let
f=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&a\\
xz&\mapsto&b\\
zy&\mapsto&c\end{array}\right)\in\ker(d_{3}^{*}).

It has been computed that
(\im d_{2}^{*}\oplus\Phi^{-2})(xz)
admits a
\mathbf{k}
-basis
R_{1}^{-2},
thus we may assume that

\begin{array}[]{rl}b=&b_{0}+\sum b_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}\\
&+\sum b_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum b_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum b_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum b_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}.\end{array}

Next, observe that
R^{-2}_{2}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
(S\oplus T)(zy)
, hence we may assume

\begin{array}[]{rl}c=&c_{0}+\sum c_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum c_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+\sum c_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+c_{7}x+\sum c_{8}(n_{1})xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum c_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum c_{5}(n_{0,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum c_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum c_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum c_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum c_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1},m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum c_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum c_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum c_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}.\end{array}

Finally, observe that
R_{3}^{-2}\cup W^{-2}_{3}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
(S^{\prime}\oplus T^{\prime})((x+y)x)
, hence we may assume

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+\sum a_{8}(n_{1})xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum a_{4}(n_{0,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{9}(n_{1,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1},m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1},m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p},m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1},m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}.\end{array}

Since
f\in\ker d_{3}^{*},
the following two equations hold:

(x+y)b-az=0\quad\text{and}\quad xc-by=0.

By plugging in the expressions of
a,b
and
c
, we can conclude that
a=b=c=0,
and consequently
f=0.

∎

Proposition 4.4
.

The Hochschild cohomology in degree
-3
vanishes.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
\ker d_{4}^{*}/\im d_{3}^{*}=0.

By Lemma
B.6
, a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
\im d_{3}^{*}(xzy)
is given by

R^{-3}_{1}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}y^{n_{0}},y^{n_{0}}x,y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}},x,xy^{n_{1}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1,}

and the vector space
\{f((x+y)xz)\mid f\in\im d_{3}^{*},f(xzy)=0\}
has a
\mathbf{k}
-basis

R^{-3}_{2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}y^{n_{0}},y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}},xy^{n_{1}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1.}

Therefore, for
f=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)xz&\mapsto&a\\
xzy&\mapsto&b\\
\end{array}\right)\in\ker(d_{4}^{*})
, we may assume that

\begin{array}[]{rl}a=&a_{0}+\sum a_{1}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}+\sum a_{2}(n_{0})y^{n_{0}}x+\sum a_{3}(n_{0},n_{1})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}+a_{7}x+\sum a_{8}(n_{1})xy^{n_{1}}\\
&+\sum a_{5}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{6}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{10}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{11}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{14}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
&+\sum a_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum a_{17}(n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
b=&b_{0}+\sum b_{12}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum b_{13}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
&+\sum b_{15}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
&+\sum b_{16}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x.\end{array}

Recall that the action of
d_{4}^{*}
on
f
takes the following form:

d_{4}^{*}(f)((x+y)xzy)=(x+y)b+ay.

Thus,
d_{4}^{*}(f)=0
gives the equation

(x+y)b+ay=0.

Plugging in the expressions of
a
and
b
, we obtain
a=b=0,
and consequently
f=0.

∎

Proposition 4.5
.

The Hochschild cohomology
\HH^{-4}(A)
has a
\mathbf{k}
-basis consisting of the following elements:

\begin{array}[]{l}A^{-4}:(x+y)xzy\mapsto 1,\\
B^{-4}(n_{0}):\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&y^{n_{0}}x,\end{array}\\
C^{-4}(n_{0,p};m_{1,p}):\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\end{array}\\
D^{-4}(n_{0,p};m_{1,p}):\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\end{array}\\
E^{-4}(n_{1,p};m_{1,p}):\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\end{array}\\
F^{-4}(n_{1,p};m_{1,p}):\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\end{array}\\
G^{-4}(n_{2,p};m_{1,p}):\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\end{array}\\
H^{-4}(n_{2,p};m_{1,p}):\begin{array}[]{ccl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\end{array}\end{array}

(17)

where
p\geqslant 1,
and
n_{i},m_{j}\geqslant 1,
for all
i
and
j.

Proof.

A direct computation shows that the elements
A^{-4},B^{-4}(\cdot),\cdots,H^{-4}(\cdot)
are linearly independent and lie in
\ker d_{5}^{*}=\Hom(V_{4},A)
(since
d_{5}^{*}=0
). Let
\Phi^{-4}\subset\ker d_{5}^{*}
be the vector subspace generated by these elements.
By Lemma
B.7
, a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
(\im d_{5}^{*})((x+y)xzy)
is given by

R^{-4}_{1}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}y^{n_{0}},y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}},x,xy^{n_{1}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}},\\
yz^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
yz^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
y^{n_{1}+1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}+xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{1}+1}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x+xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1,}

and for
\Phi^{-4}((x+y)xzy)
is given by

W^{-4}_{1}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1,y^{n_{0}}x,\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}},\\
z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x,\end{array}\right\}_{p\geqslant 1,n_{0},\cdots,n_{p+1},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1.}

Therefore
R^{-4}_{1}\cap W^{-4}_{1}=\emptyset
implies that
\im d_{4}^{*}\cap\Phi^{-4}=0.

It remains to show that
\im d_{4}^{*}\oplus\Phi^{-4}=\ker d_{5}^{*}=\Hom(V_{4},A)
.
By Corollary
2.3
, the leftmost terms of elements in
U^{-4}_{1}
and
W^{-4}_{1}
form a
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
A
,
which completes the proof.

∎

Since the cochain complex (
9
) of Koszul resolution of
A
vanishes in degrees less than or equal to
-5
, we have the following conclusion.

Corollary 4.6
.

For
n\geqslant 5,
the Hochschild cohomology
\HH^{-n}(A)
vanishes.

5
Cup Product

Proposition
4.4
shows that
\HH^{-3}(A)=0
. Since the cup product is of degree
0
, there are components where the cup product is automatically zero:

Corollary 5.1
.

The cup product
\cup_{-m,-n}:\HH^{-m}(A)\otimes\HH^{-n}(A)\to\HH^{-m-n}(A)
is zero for
(m,n)=(1,2),(2,1),(1,3),(3,1)
and for all
(m,n)
such that
m+n>4
. If
m=0
or
n=0
, then
\cup_{-m,-n}=\id_{\HH^{-n}(A)}
or
\id_{\HH^{-m}(A)}
, respectively.

Moreover, in our case, the remaining cup products are also trivial.

Proposition 5.2
.

The cup product
\cup:\HH^{*}(A)\otimes\HH^{*}(A)\to\HH^{*}(A)
is zero except for
\cup_{-m,0}=\id_{\HH^{-m}(A)}
or
\cup_{0,-n}=\id_{\HH^{-n}(A)}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.1
and the graded commutativity of the cup product, we only need to show that
\cup_{-1,-1}
and
\cup_{-2,-2}
are zero. Recall that the Koszul Hochschild cochain complex is given by
C^{-n}(A)=\Hom(V_{n},A)
. In
[
4
, Section 3]
, Berger, Lambre and Solotar gave a formula of the cup product on the level of Koszul cochains:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\cup_{K}:C^{-m}(A)\otimes C^{-n}(A)&\to&C^{-m-n}(A)\\
f\otimes g&\mapsto&\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a_{1}\cdots a_{m+n}\mapsto(-1)^{mn}f(a_{1}\cdots a_{m})\cdot g(a_{m+1}\cdots a_{m+n})\\
\end{array}\right)\end{array}

where
m,n\geqslant 0
,
a_{1},\cdots,a_{m+n}\in\mathbf{k}\{x,y,z\}
,
a_{1}\cdots a_{m+n}\in V_{m+n}
,
a_{1}\cdots a_{m}\in V_{m}
and
a_{m+1}\cdots a_{m+n}\in V_{n}
.

We first show that
X^{-1}\cup Y^{-1}=0
for
X^{-1},Y^{-1}\in\{A^{-1},B^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}\}
where
A^{-1},B^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}
are cohomology classes in
\HH^{-1}(A)
as in Proposition
4.2
; when no confusion arises, we suppress the parameters in
B^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}
. Recall that
V_{2}=\{(x+y)x,xz,zy\}
. We have

A^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&(-1)^{1}A^{-1}(x+y)\cdot X^{-1}(x)\\
xz&\mapsto&(-1)^{1}A^{-1}(x)\cdot X^{-1}(z)=0\\
zy&\mapsto&(-1)^{1}A^{-1}(z)\cdot X^{-1}(y)=0\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(x+y)\cdot X^{-1}(x)\\
xz&\mapsto&-x\cdot X^{-1}(z)\\
zy&\mapsto&-0\cdot X^{-1}(y)=0\end{array}\right).

So
A^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0
if
X^{-1}(x)=X^{-1}(z)=0
. Therefore,

A^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=D^{-1},E^{-1},F^{-1},G^{-1},H^{-1},I^{-1}.

For the rests:

A^{-1}\cup A^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(x+y)\cdot x=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-x\cdot 0=0\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)=0,

A^{-1}\cup B^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(x+y)\cdot y^{n}x=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-x\cdot 0\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)=0,

A^{-1}\cup C^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(x+y)\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-x\cdot zxy^{n}=0\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)=0,

A^{-1}\cup J^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(x+y)\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&x\cdot(zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}-1})=0\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)=0,

A^{-1}\cup K^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(x+y)\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-x\cdot(z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}})=0\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)=0.

Since

B^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-0\cdot X^{-1}(x)=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-y^{n}x\cdot X^{-1}(z)\\
zy&\mapsto&-0\cdot X^{-1}(y)=0\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&-y^{n}xX^{-1}(z)\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),

B^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0
if
X^{-1}(z)=0
. Hence we have

B^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=B^{-1},D^{-1},E^{-1},F^{-1},G^{-1},H^{-1},I^{-1}.

For the rest:

B^{-1}\cup C^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&-y^{n}x\cdot zxy^{n}=0\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)=0,

B^{-1}\cup J^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&y^{n}x\cdot zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}-1}=0\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)=0,

B^{-1}\cup K^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&-y^{n}x\cdot(z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}})=0\\
zy&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)=0,

For
C^{-1}
we have

C^{-1}\cup C^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot zxy^{n}=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})=0\end{array}\right)=0,

C^{-1}\cup D^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot 0=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(y^{n}x-y^{i}xy^{n-i})=0\end{array}\right)=0,

C^{-1}\cup F^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot 0=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(y^{n}_{0}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x)=0\end{array}\right)=0,

C^{-1}\cup H^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot 0=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}})=0\end{array}\right)=0,

C^{-1}\cup H^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot 0=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}})=0\end{array}\right)=0,

C^{-1}\cup J^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\cdot(zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}-1})=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}})=0\end{array}\right)=0,

C^{-1}\cup K^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\cdot(z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}})=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot 0=0\end{array}\right)=0.

The elements
C^{-1}\cup E^{-1},C^{-1}\cup G^{-1}
and
C^{-1}\cup I^{-1}
are not directly zero but in the image of
d_{2}^{*}
:

\begin{array}[]{rl}C^{-1}\cup E^{-1}&=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot 0=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(y^{n}x+y^{n+1})=-zxy^{2n+1}\end{array}\right)\\
&=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{2n+1}\end{array}\right)=d_{2}^{*}\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&0\\
z&\mapsto&-zxy^{2n}\\
\end{array}\right),\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rl}C^{-1}\cup G^{-1}&=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot 0=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x)\end{array}\right)\\
&=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n+n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\end{array}\right)\\
&=d_{2}^{*}\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&-xy^{n+n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rl}C^{-1}\cup I^{-1}&=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot 0=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n}\cdot(y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}})\end{array}\right)\\
&=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\\
zy&\mapsto&-zxy^{n+n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\end{array}\right)\\
&=d_{2}^{*}\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&0\\
y&\mapsto&-xy^{n+n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}\\
z&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right),\end{array}

hence also vanish in
\HH^{-2}(A)
. Next

D^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-D^{1}(x+y)\cdot X^{-1}(x)\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot X^{-1}(z)=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-0\cdot X^{-1}(y)=0\end{array}\right)

Since
X^{-1}(x)=0
for
X^{-1}=D^{-1},E^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}
, we have

D^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=D^{-1},E^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}.

Now for
E^{-1}
, we have

E^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-E^{-1}(x+y)\cdot X^{-1}(x)\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot X^{-1}(z)=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-0\cdot X^{-1}(y)=0\end{array}\right).

Since
X^{-1}(x)=0
for
X^{-1}=E^{-1},F^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}
, we have

E^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=E^{-1},F^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}

By the exact same reason, we also have

F^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=F^{-1},G^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1},

G^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=G^{-1},H^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1},

H^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=H^{-1},I^{-1},J^{-1},K^{-1},

I^{-1}\cup X^{-1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=I^{-1},J^{-1},K^{-1}.

It remains to compute that

J^{-1}\cup J^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-J^{-1}(x+y)\cdot 0=0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot J^{-1}(z)=0\\
zy&\mapsto&(zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}-1})\cdot(xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}})=0\end{array}\right)=0,

J^{-1}\cup K^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-J^{-1}(x+y)\cdot 0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot K^{-1}(z)=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-J^{-1}(z)\cdot 0=0\end{array}\right)=0,

K^{-1}\cup K^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&-0\cdot 0\\
xz&\mapsto&-0\cdot K^{-1}(z)=0\\
zy&\mapsto&-K^{-1}(z)\cdot 0=0\end{array}\right)=0.

By the graded commutativity, we conclude that
\cup_{-1,-1}:\HH^{-1}(A)\otimes\HH^{-1}(A)\to\HH^{-2}(A)
vanishes.

Next we compute
\cup_{2,2}
. Recall that
V_{4}=\{(x+y)xzy\}
, and
A^{-2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&x\\
zy&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)
and
B^{-2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)x&\mapsto&z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
zy&\mapsto&0\\
xz&\mapsto&0\end{array}\right)
are the two cohomology classes of
\HH^{-2}(A)
, see Proposition
4.3
. We have

A^{-2}\cup A^{-2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&(-1)^{4}A^{-2}((x+y)x\cdot A^{-2}(zy)=x\cdot 0=0\end{array}\right)=0

A^{-2}\cup B^{-2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&x\cdot 0=0\end{array}\right)=0

B^{-2}\cup B^{-2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x+y)xzy&\mapsto&B^{-2}((x+y)x)\cdot 0=0\end{array}\right)=0

By the graded commutativity, the cup product
\cup_{-2,-2}:\HH^{-2}(A)\otimes\HH^{-2}(A)\to\HH^{-4}(A)
also vanishes.
∎

6
Cap Product

By Section
3
and Proposition
4.1
, most of the cap products are trivial.

Corollary 6.1
.

\cap_{n}^{-m}:\HH^{-m}(A)\otimes\HH_{n}(A)\to\HH_{n-m}(A)
is zero for
(m,n)\neq(0,1),(0,0)
or
(1,0)
. Moreover,
\cap_{n}^{0}=\id_{\HH_{n}(A)}
for any
n\geqslant 0
. The only nontrivial cap product is

\cap_{1}^{-1}:\HH^{-1}(A)\otimes\HH_{1}(A)\to\HH_{0}(A).

Recall from Proposition
3.4
,
3.5
and
4.2
that the cohomology classes in
\HH^{-1}(A)
are labeled by
A^{-1},\cdots K^{-1}
, the homology classes in
\HH_{0}(A)
are labeled by
\alpha_{0},\cdots,\epsilon_{0}
, and the homology classes in
\HH_{1}(A)
are labeled by
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\theta_{1}
. Recall also from Remark
2.4
that we use
n_{i,\cdots,j}
to denote
n_{i},\cdots,n_{j}
for
i\leqslant j
and similarly for
m_{i,\cdots,j}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

The cap product
\cap_{1}^{-1}
is given by

\rhd

A^{-1}\cap\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{0},\quad B^{-1}(n)\cap\alpha_{1}=\beta_{0}(n),

\rhd

A^{-1}\cap\beta_{1}=-\beta_{0}(1),\quad B^{-1}(n)\cap\beta_{1}=-\beta_{0}(n+1),

\rhd

A^{-1}\cap\gamma_{1}(n_{0})=-\beta_{0}(n_{0}+1),\quad B^{-1}(n)\cap\gamma_{1}(n_{0})=-\beta_{0}(n_{0}+n+1),

\rhd

A^{-1}\cap\delta_{1}=\gamma_{0}(1),\quad B^{-1}(n)\cap\delta_{1}=-\beta_{0}(n),

\rhd

E^{-1}(n)\cap\delta_{1}=\beta_{0}(n)+\gamma_{0}(n+1),

\rhd

G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\delta_{1}=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\delta_{1}=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1}+n_{p+1},n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

A^{-1}\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=\gamma_{0}(n_{0}+1),\quad B^{-1}(n)\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=-\gamma_{0}(n_{0}+n),

\rhd

E^{-1}(n)\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=\beta_{0}(n_{0}+n)+\gamma_{0}(n_{0}+n+1),

\rhd

G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{0}+n_{1},n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{0}+n_{1}+n_{p+1},n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\zeta_{1}=-\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{p+1}-1,n_{1,\cdots,p},1;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\zeta_{1}=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{2,\cdots,p-1},m_{p}+m_{1}),

\rhd

C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\eta_{1}(n_{1})=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n,n_{1}+1),

\rhd

J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\eta_{1}(n_{1}^{\prime})=-\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{p+1}-1,n_{1}^{\prime}+1,n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\eta_{1}(n_{1})=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{2,\cdots,p-1},m_{p}+m_{1}+1),

\rhd

A^{-1}\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}(n_{i}+1)\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i},n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s,m_{i}-s,m_{i+1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

E^{-1}(n)\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}n_{i}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},n_{i}+n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

G^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 17.07164pt=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},t+r_{1}-1,r_{2,\cdots,q},n_{i}-t,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{i,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

I^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 17.07164pt=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},t+r_{1}-1,r_{2,\cdots,q},n_{i}+r_{q+1}-t,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{i,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

J^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 17.07164pt=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i},r_{1,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}-1,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s,s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{i}-s,m_{i+1,\cdots,p}),

\rhd

K^{-1}(r_{2,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i},r_{2,\cdots,p},n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s+s_{1}-1,s_{2},\cdots,s_{p-1},m_{i}+s_{p}-s,m_{i+1,\cdots,p})

Proof.

Recall that the (co)homology classes in
\HH^{-1}(A)
and
\HH_{1}(A)
are given in Propositions
4.2
and
3.4
. A formula of the cap product is given in
[
4
]
by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\cap_{K}:C^{m}(A)\otimes C_{n}(A)&\to&C_{n-m}(A)\\
f\otimes(a_{0}\otimes a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})&\mapsto&a_{0}f(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{m})\cdot a_{m+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n},\end{array}

which is defined on the Koszul Hochschild chain complexes
C_{n}=A\otimes V_{n}
and cochain complexes
C^{m}=\Hom(V_{n},A)
and induces the same cap product as in Definition
A.12
on
\HH^{*}(A)\otimes\HH_{*}(A)
. Here the letters
