a_{1},\cdots,a_{m+n}\in\{x,y,z\}
, satisfying
a_{1}\cdots a_{m+n}\in V_{m+n}
,
a_{1}\cdots a_{m}\in V_{m}
and
a_{m+1}\cdots a_{m+n}\in V_{n}
.

For a class
X^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{rcl}x&\mapsto&X^{-1}(x)\\
y&\mapsto&X^{-1}(y)\\
z&\mapsto&X^{-1}(z)\end{array}\right)\in\HH^{-1}(A)
and a class
\chi_{1}=a\otimes x+b\otimes y+c\otimes z\in\HH_{1}(A)
, we have

X^{-1}\cap\chi_{1}=aX^{-1}(x)+bX^{-1}(y)+cX^{-1}(z)

where the products are computed in
A
.

Case 1: suppose
\chi_{1}=\alpha_{1}=1\otimes x
. Then

X^{-1}\cap\chi_{1}=X^{-1}(x).

Since
X^{-1}(x)=0
for all
X^{-1}\in\HH^{-1}(A)
except for
X^{-1}=A^{-1},B^{-1}
, we have

X^{-1}\cap\alpha_{1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=C^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}.

and

A^{-1}\cap\alpha_{1}=A^{-1}(x)=x=\alpha_{0}\in\HH_{0}(A);

B^{-1}(n)\cap\alpha_{1}=B^{-1}(x)=y^{n}x=\beta_{0}(n).

Case 2:
\chi_{1}=\beta_{1}=x\otimes x
. Then

X^{-1}\cap\chi_{1}=xX^{-1}(x).

So

X^{-1}\cap\beta_{1}=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=C^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}

for the same reason. We also have

A^{-1}\cap\beta_{1}=xA^{-1}(x)=x^{2}=-yx=-\beta_{0}(1);

B^{-1}(n)\cap\beta_{1}=xB^{-1}(x)=xy^{n}x=-y^{n+1}x=-\beta_{0}(n+1).

Case 3:
\chi_{1}=\gamma_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}x\otimes x

(n_{0}\geqslant 2)
:

X^{-1}\cap\chi_{1}=y^{n_{0}}xX^{-1}(x).

So we still have

X^{-1}\cap\gamma_{1}(n_{0})=0\quad\text{for}\quad X^{-1}=C^{-1},\cdots,K^{-1}

and

A^{-1}\cap\gamma_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}x\cdot x=-y^{n_{0}+1}x=-\beta_{0}(n_{0}+1);

B^{-1}(n)\cap\gamma_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}x\cdot y^{n}x=-y^{n_{0}+n+1}x=-\beta_{0}(n_{0}+n+1).

Case 4:
\chi_{1}=\delta_{1}=1\otimes y
, then

X^{-1}\cap\chi_{1}=X^{-1}(y).

So

K^{-1}\cap\delta_{1}=0

since
K^{-1}(y)=0
. For the rest,

A^{-1}\cap\delta_{1}=y=\gamma_{0}(1);

B^{-1}(n)\cap\delta_{1}=-y^{n}x=-\beta_{0}(n)

C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\delta_{1}=y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1}=d_{1}(y^{n+1}\otimes x)

i.e.,
C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\delta_{1}
is a degree
0
boundary, whence

C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\delta_{1}=0;

D^{-1}(n,i)\cap\delta_{1}=y^{n}x-y^{i}xy^{n-i}=d_{1}(y^{n}\otimes x+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{i-1}y^{j}xy^{n-j-1}\otimes y)

i.e.
D^{-1}(n,i)\cap\delta_{1}
is a degree
0
boundary chain, whence

D^{-1}(n,i)\cap\delta_{1}=0;

E^{-1}(n)\cap\delta_{1}=y^{n}x+y^{n+1}=\beta_{0}(n)+\gamma_{0}(n+1);

F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})f\cap\delta_{1}=y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x

which is homologous to
0
by Remark
3.6
, hence

F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\delta_{1}=0;

G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\delta_{1}=y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p});

H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\delta_{1}=y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}=0

by Remark
3.6
(we will keep using Remark
3.6
without recalling in the following computations);

\begin{array}[]{l}I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\delta_{1}=\\
y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}=y^{n_{1}+n_{p+1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1}+n_{p+1},n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\end{array}

J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\delta_{1}=xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}=0;

Case 5:
\chi_{1}=\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}\otimes y
, then

X^{-1}\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}X^{-1}(y).

Since
K^{-1}(y)=0
, we have

K^{-1}\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=0.

A^{-1}\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}+1}=\gamma_{0}(n_{0}+1);

B^{-1}(n)\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}(-y^{n}x)=-y^{n_{0}+n}x=-\gamma_{0}(n_{0}+n);

C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})=y^{n_{0}+n+1}x-y^{n_{0}}xy^{n+1}=0;

D^{-1}(n,i)\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}(y^{n}x-y^{i}xy^{n-i})=y^{n_{0}+n}x-y^{n_{0}+i}xy^{n-i}=0;

E^{-1}(n)\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}}(y^{n}x+y^{n+1})=y^{n_{0}+n}x+y^{n_{0}+n+1}=\beta_{0}(n_{0}+n)+\gamma_{0}(n_{0}+n+1);

F^{-1}(n_{0}^{\prime},n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}+n_{0}^{\prime}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=0;

G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}+n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{0}+n_{1},n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p});

H^{-1}(n_{0}^{\prime},n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})=y^{n_{0}+n_{0}^{\prime}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots y^{n_{p+1}}=0;

\begin{array}[]{l}I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\epsilon_{1}(n_{0})\\
=y^{n_{0}+n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}=y^{n_{0}+n_{1}+n_{p+1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{0}+n_{1}+n_{p+1},n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p});\end{array}

J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})=y^{n_{0}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}}=0;

Case 6:
\chi_{1}=\zeta_{1}=1\otimes z
, then

X^{-1}\cap\chi_{1}=X^{-1}(z),

Since
X^{-1}(z)=0
for
X^{-1}=A^{-1},B^{-1},D^{-1},E^{-1},F^{-1},G^{-1},H^{-1},I^{-1}
,
X^{-1}\cap\zeta_{1}=0
for these cohomology classes. We have also

C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\zeta_{1}=zxy^{n}=y^{n}zx=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n,1);

\begin{array}[]{l}J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\zeta_{1}\\
=-zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}-1}=-y^{n_{p+1}-1}zxy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
=-\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{p+1}-1,n_{1,\cdots,p},1;m_{1,\cdots,p});\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\zeta_{1}\\
=z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}=y^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p-1}}xy^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}+m_{1}}x\\
=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{2,\cdots,p-1},m_{p}+m_{1});\end{array}

Case 7:
\chi_{1}=\eta_{1}(n_{1})=z^{n_{1}}\otimes z
, then

X^{-1}\cap\chi_{1}=z^{n_{1}}X^{-1}(z)

So
X^{-1}\cap\eta_{1}(n_{1})=0
for
X^{-1}=A^{-1},B^{-1},D^{-1},E^{-1},F^{-1},G^{-1},H^{-1},I^{-1}
for the same reason.

C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\eta_{1}(n_{1})=z^{n_{1}+1}xy^{n}=y^{n}z^{n_{1}+1}x=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n,n_{1}+1);

\begin{array}[]{l}J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\eta_{1}(n_{1}^{\prime})\\
=-z^{n_{1}^{\prime}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{p+1}-1}=-y^{n_{p+1}-1}z^{n_{1}^{\prime}+1}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
=-\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{p+1}-1,n_{1}^{\prime}+1,n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p});\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\cap\eta_{1}(n_{1})\\
=z^{m_{1}+1}xy^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}=y^{n_{2}}z^{m_{2}}\cdots z^{m_{p-1}}xy^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}+m_{1}+1}x\\
=\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{2,\cdots,p},m_{2,\cdots,p-1},m_{p}+m_{1}+1);\end{array}

Case 8:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\chi_{1}=\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=&(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}})\otimes x\\
&+(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\otimes y\\
&+(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s-1})\otimes z\end{array}

Then

\begin{array}[]{rl}X^{-1}\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=&(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}})\cdot X^{-1}(x)\\
&+(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot X^{-1}(y)\\
&+(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s-1})\cdot X^{-1}(z).\end{array}

We have

\begin{array}[]{rl}A^{-1}\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=&(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}})\cdot x\\
&+(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot y\\
=&(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}x)\\
&+(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}x)\\
=&(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}(n_{i}+1)y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}x)\\
=&\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}(n_{i}+1)\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rl}B^{-1}\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=&(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}})\cdot y^{n}x\\
&+(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(-y^{n}x)\\
=&0+0=0;\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}C^{-1}(n+1)\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\\
(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}x\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(y^{n+1}x-xy^{n+1})\\
+(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s-1})\cdot zxy^{n}\\
=0+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}y^{n}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s}x\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s}xy^{n}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i},n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s,m_{i}-s,m_{i+1,\cdots,p})\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}D^{-1}(n,i)\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\\
(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(y^{n}x-y^{i}xy^{n-i})=0\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}D^{-1}(n,i)\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})=\\
(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(y^{n}x-y^{i}xy^{n-i})=0\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}E^{-1}(n)\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\\
=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(y^{n}x+y^{n+1})\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t+n}\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}+n}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}x\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}n_{i}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},n_{i}+n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}F^{-1}(r_{0,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\\
=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(y^{r_{0}}xy^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}x)=0\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}G^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\\
=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(y^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}x)\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1}y^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}xy^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},t+r_{1}-1,r_{2,\cdots,q},n_{i}-t,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{i,\cdots,p})\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}H^{-1}(r_{0,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\\
=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(y^{r_{0}}xy^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}xy^{r_{q+1}})\\
=0\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}I^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,n_{p}};m_{1,\cdots,p})\\
=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(y^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}xy^{r_{q+1}})\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1}y^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}xy^{n_{i}+r_{q+1}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},t+r_{1}-1,r_{2,\cdots,q},n_{i}+r_{q+1}-t,n_{i+1,\cdots,n_{p}};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{i,\cdots,p})\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}J^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\\
=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{t-1})\cdot(xy^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}xy^{r_{q+1}})\\
-(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s-1})\cdot(zxy^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}xy^{r_{q+1}-1})\\
=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s}xy^{r_{1}}z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}xy^{r_{q+1}-1}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}\\
=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i},r_{1,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}-1,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s,s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{i}-s,m_{i+1,\cdots,p})\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}K^{-1}(r_{2,\cdots,q},s_{1,\cdots,q})\cap\theta_{1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})\\
=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s-1})\cdot(z^{s_{1}}\cdots z^{s_{q}})\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}z^{m_{i}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s+s_{1}-1}xy^{r_{2}}z^{s_{2}}\cdots z^{s_{q}}\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}\cdots z^{m_{i-1}}xy^{n_{i}}z^{s+s_{1}-1}xy^{r_{2}}z^{s_{2}}\cdots z^{s_{q-1}}xy^{r_{q}}z^{m_{i}+s_{q}-s}xy^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}\cdots z^{m_{p}}x\\
=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{m_{i}}\overline{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1,\cdots,i},r_{2,\cdots,p},n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,i-1},s+s_{1}-1,s_{2},\cdots,s_{p-1},m_{i}+s_{p}-s,m_{i+1,\cdots,p})\end{array}

∎

7
Comparison morphisms

In previous sections, we have realized the Hochschild homology and cohomology of
A
as the homology groups of
A\otimes_{A^{e}}K_{\bullet}
and
\Hom_{A^{e}}(K_{\bullet},A)
obtained through the Koszul resolution of
A
, respectively.
However, the Tamarkin–Tsygan calculus of
A
is defined on the homology groups of
A\otimes_{A^{e}}B(A,A)
and
\Hom_{A^{e}}(B(A,A),A)
obtained through the two-sided bar resolution of
A
, see Appendix
A
. The cup product and cap product can be defined directly on the Koszul resolution of
A
as explained in
[
4
]
, which allows us to calculate directly in Sections
5
and
6
. In contrast, similarly simple formulas for the Connes’ differential and the Gerstenhaber bracket are not available in the literature. Therefore, the comparison morphisms between these two resolutions are required.

In this section, we will construct the comparison morphisms between the Koszul resolution of
A
and the two-sided bar resolution of
A
via algebraic Morse theory
[
7
,
16
]
.
We begin by recalling the key notations and main results of algebraic Morse theory from the primary reference
[
7
]
, with minor modifications for simplicity.

Let
(X_{\bullet},d_{\bullet})
be a complex of vector spaces.
Suppose that for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, there exists a decomposition into direct sums of subspaces

X_{n}=\oplus_{i\in I_{n}}X_{n,i}.

So
d_{n}:X_{n}\to X_{n-1}
has a matrix presentation
d_{n}=(d_{n,ji})
with
i\in I_{n},j\in I_{n-1}.

We shall construct a weighted quiver
Q=Q_{X_{\bullet}}
as follows:

(Q1)

The vertices are the pairs
(n,i)
with
n\in\mathbb{Z},i\in I_{n}
;

(Q2)

if a map
d_{n,ji}
with
i\in I_{n},j\in I_{n-1}
does not vanish, then draw an arrow from
(n,i)
to
(n-1,j)
;

(Q3)

for an arrow in (Q2), its weight is just the map
d_{n,ji}
.

A
partial matching
is a full subquiver
{\mathcal{M}}
of
Q
such that

(M1)

each vertex in
Q
belongs to at most one arrow of
{\mathcal{M}}
;

(M2)

each arrow in
{\mathcal{M}}
has its weight invertible as a linear maps.

Given a partial matching
{\mathcal{M}}
, we can construct a new weighted quiver
Q^{\mathcal{M}}
with additional dotted arrows as follows:

(QM1)

Keep everything for all arrows which are not in
{\mathcal{M}}
(they will be called
thick arrows
);

(QM2)

For an arrow in
{\mathcal{M}}
, replace it by a new
dotted arrow
in the reverse direction and the weight of this new arrow is the negative inverse of the weight of the original arrow.

A vertex of
Q
a
critical vertex
(with respect to
{\mathcal{M}}
), if it is not incident to any arrow in
{\mathcal{M}}
, and
a path in
Q^{\mathcal{M}}
is called
zigzag
if dotted arrows and thick arrows appear alternately.
We adopt the following notations:

{\mathcal{V}}_{n}

=\{(n,i)\mid i\in I_{n}\}

{\mathcal{U}}_{n}

=\{(n,i)\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\mid(n,i)\xrightarrow{d_{n,ji}}(n-1,j)\in{\mathcal{M}}\}

{\mathcal{D}}_{n}

=\{(n-1,j)\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\mid(n,i)\xrightarrow{d_{n,ji}}(n-1,j)\in{\mathcal{M}}\}

{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathcal{M}}

the set of critical vertices

\varphi_{p}^{\mathcal{M}}

=w_{n}\circ w_{n-1}\circ\cdots\circ w_{1},
for
p:\bullet\xrightarrow{w_{1}}\bullet\cdots\bullet\xrightarrow{w_{n}}\bullet

{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{M}}((n,i),(m,j))

the set of all zigzag paths from
(n,i)
to
(m,j)
in
Q^{\mathcal{M}}

A
Morse matching
is a partial matching which satisfies the
local finiteness hypothesis
in
[
7
, Section 3]
called (LFH).

Given a Morse matching
{\mathcal{M}}
, we can construct a new complex (called
Morse complex
)
({X}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}},d_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}})
as follows:

The complex
{X}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}}
has its
n
-th component
X_{n}^{\mathcal{M}}=\oplus_{(n,i)\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathcal{M}}}X_{n,i}
and the differential

d_{n}^{\mathcal{M}}:X_{n}^{\mathcal{M}}\to X_{n-1}^{\mathcal{M}}
has the matrix presentation

d_{n}^{\mathcal{M}}=(d_{n,ji}^{\mathcal{M}})
with
(n,i)\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathcal{M}},(n-1,j)\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n-1}^{\mathcal{M}}
and where

d_{n,ji}^{\mathcal{M}}:X_{n,i}\to X_{n-1,j}
is defined to be

d_{n,ji}^{\mathcal{M}}=\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{M}}((n,i),(n-1,j))}\varphi^{\mathcal{M}}_{p}.

The main theorem of algebraic Morse theory is as follows.

Theorem 7.1

(
[
7
]
)
.

(i)

Within the above setup,

({X}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}},d_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}})
is a complex.

(ii)

Define maps

\displaystyle f_{n}:X_{n}^{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow X_{n}

\displaystyle x\in X_{n,i}

\displaystyle\mapsto f_{n}(x):=x+\sum_{(n,j)\in{\mathcal{U}}_{n}}\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{M}}((n,i),(n,j))}\varphi^{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(x),

and

\displaystyle g_{n}:X_{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow X^{\mathcal{M}}_{n}

\displaystyle x\in X_{n,i}

\displaystyle\mapsto g_{n}(x):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{(n,j)\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathcal{M}}_{n}}\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{M}}((n,i),(n,j))}\varphi^{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(x),&(n,i)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{n}\\
x,&(n,i)\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathcal{M}}\\
0&(n,i)\in{\mathcal{U}}_{n}.\end{array}\right.

Then
f_{\bullet}:{X}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow X_{\bullet}
and
g_{\bullet}:X_{\bullet}\rightarrow{X}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}}
are chain maps which are homotopy equivalent:

gf=\mathrm{Id}_{{X}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}}}
and
fg\sim\mathrm{Id}_{X_{\bullet}}
via the homotopy

\displaystyle{\theta_{n}}:X_{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow X_{n+1}

\displaystyle x\in X_{n,i}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\theta_{n}}(x):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{(n+1,j)\in{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}}\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{M}}((n,i),(n+1,j))}\varphi^{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(x),&(n,i)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{n}\\
0&otherwise.\end{array}\right.

(iii)

We have a decomposition

(X_{\bullet},d_{\bullet})\cong(X_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}},d_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{M}})\oplus(Y_{\bullet},d_{\bullet}^{Y})

where
(Y_{\bullet},d_{\bullet}^{Y})
is a null homotopic complex.

Next, we consider the following direct sum decomposition of the reduced two-sided bar resolution
B(A,A)
of
A
:

B(A,A)_{n}=A\otimes\overline{A}^{\otimes n}\otimes A\cong\bigoplus_{w_{1},\cdots,w_{n}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{+}}A\otimes\mathbf{k}\{(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\}\otimes A,\quad\text{for}~n\geqslant 1,

where
{\mathcal{B}}_{+}
is the
\mathbf{k}
-basis of
\overline{A}
(see Appendix
A
) defined in Corollary
2.3
.
This decomposition gives rise to a weighted quiver, which we denote by
Q_{B}.

In practical computations, it is more convenient to use the quiver
\overline{Q_{B}}
introduced in
[
7
, Section 4]
.

Lets describe the quiver
\overline{Q_{B}}
induced by this decomposition more concretely.
The vertices of
\overline{Q_{B}}
corresponding to the direct summands

A\otimes\mathbf{k}\{(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\}\otimes A,~n\geqslant 1,w_{1},\cdots,w_{n}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{+},

B_{0}=A\otimes A,
and
B_{-1}=A

are denoted by

(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n}),~\ast_{0},
and
\ast_{-1},
respectively.
In the quiver
\overline{Q_{B}},
fix a vertex
(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n}).
For
n\geqslant 1:

(i)

For
1\leqslant i\leqslant n,
let
d_{n}^{i}
be the arrow (with weight written above it)

(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\xrightarrow{(-1)^{i}\lambda\otimes 1}(w_{1},\cdots,w_{i-1},u,w_{i+2},\cdots,w_{n}),

where either
w_{i}w_{i+1}=u\in{\mathcal{B}}_{+}
or
w_{i}w_{i+1}
reduces to
u
. In other words,
w_{i}w_{i+1}

reduces to

u
in the algebra
A
means that

w_{i}w_{i+1}-\lambda u+\text{other terms}\equiv 0\text{~in~}A,

where
\lambda\in\mathbf{k}\setminus\{0\},
and
w_{i}w_{i+1}
is the leading term under the given monomial order.

(ii)

Let
d_{n}^{0}
and
d_{n}^{n}
denote the arrows

(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes 1}(w_{2},\cdots,w_{n}),

(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\xrightarrow{(-1)^{n}\otimes w_{n}}(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n-1}),

Finally, let
d_{0}^{0}
denotes the arrow
\ast_{0}\xrightarrow{1}\ast_{-1}.

In
[
16
]
, Sköldberg constructed the following Morse matching
{\mathcal{M}}
such that the Morse complex
B(A,A)^{{\mathcal{M}}}
is exactly the two-sided Anick resolution of
A
.

Proposition 7.2

(
[
16
]
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be a full subquiver of
\overline{Q_{B}}
consisting of the following arrows

(i)

d_{n+1}^{1}:(x,u_{1},\cdots,u_{n})\xrightarrow{-1\otimes 1}(xu_{1},\cdots,u_{n});

(ii)

d_{n+1}^{1}:(y,v_{1},\cdots,v_{n})\xrightarrow{-1\otimes 1}(yv_{1},\cdots,v_{n});

(iii)

d_{n+1}^{1}:(z,w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\xrightarrow{-1\otimes 1}(zw_{1},\cdots,w_{n});

(iv)

d_{n+m+1}^{n+1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,u_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,u_{m}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(-1)^{n+1}\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}xu_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,u_{m}^{\prime});

(v)

d_{n+m+1}^{n+1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z,v_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,v_{m}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(-1)^{n+1}\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,zv_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,v_{m}^{\prime});

(vi)

d_{n+m+1}^{n+1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}}x,z,y,w_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,w_{m}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(-1)^{n+1}\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z,yw_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,w_{m}^{\prime}),

for
m,n\geqslant 1,k_{1},\cdots,k_{n}\geqslant 0,
and
xu_{1},yv_{1},zw_{1},y^{k_{n}}xu_{1}^{\prime},zv_{1}^{\prime},yw_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{+}.

Then
{\mathcal{M}}
is a Morse matching such that the Morse complex
B(A,A)^{{\mathcal{M}}}
is the two-sided Anick resolution of
A
.

In
[
9
]
, Dotsenko and Roy Chowdhury computed the Anick resolution of
A
. Later, in
[
7
]
, the two-sided Anick resolution of
A
was obtained via the Morse matching due to Sköldberg.

Proposition 7.3

(
[
7
]
,
[
9
]
)
.

The two-sided Anick resolution
P
of
A
with respect to the monomial order generated by
z<y<x
is given by has the form

\cdots\to A\otimes\mathbf{k}W^{(n)}\otimes A\xrightarrow{\partial_{n}}A\otimes\mathbf{k}W^{(n-1)}\otimes A\to\cdots\to A\otimes\mathbf{k}W^{(0)}\otimes A\xrightarrow{\partial_{1}}A\otimes A\xrightarrow{\partial_{0}}A\to 0,

where

(i)

W^{(0)}=\mathbf{k}\{x,y,z\}
;

(ii)

W^{(1)}=\mathbf{k}\{(x,y^{k}x),(x,z),(z,y)\mid~(k\geqslant 0)\}
;

(iii)

W^{(n)}=\mathbf{k}\{(x,y^{k_{1}}x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x),(x,y^{k_{1}}x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z),(x,y^{k_{1}}x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}}x,z,y)\allowbreak\mid\allowbreak k_{1}\allowbreak,\cdots\allowbreak,k_{n}\allowbreak\geqslant 0\}
for
n\geqslant 2
.

Its differential is given by the following equation.

(i)

\partial_{0}(1\otimes 1)=1;

(ii)

\partial_{1}(1\otimes a\otimes 1)=a\otimes 1-1\otimes a,

a=x,y,z;

(iii)

\partial_{2}(1\otimes(x,y^{k}x)\otimes 1)=(xy^{k}+y^{k+1})\otimes x\otimes 1+1\otimes x\otimes y^{k}x+1\otimes y\otimes y^{k}x+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{k}(xy^{i-1}+y^{i})\otimes y\otimes y^{k-i}x,\\
\partial_{2}(1\otimes(x,z)\otimes 1)=x\otimes z\otimes 1+1\otimes x\otimes z,\\
\partial_{2}(1\otimes(z,y)\otimes 1)=z\otimes y\otimes 1+1\otimes z\otimes y;

(iv)

for
n\geqslant 2,

\partial_{n+1}(1\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x)\otimes 1)

\begin{split}=&(xy^{k_{1}}+y^{k_{1}+1})\otimes(x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x)\otimes 1+(-1)^{n+1}\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x)\otimes y^{k_{n}}x\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{i}\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{i-1}}x,y^{k_{i}+k_{i+1}+1}x,y^{k_{i+2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x)\otimes 1,\end{split}

\partial_{n+1}(1\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z)\otimes 1)

\begin{split}=&(xy^{k_{1}}+y^{k_{1}+1})\otimes(x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z)\otimes 1+(-1)^{n+1}\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x)\otimes z\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}(-1)^{i}\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{i-1}}x,y^{k_{i}+k_{i+1}+1}x,y^{k_{i+2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z)\otimes 1,\end{split}

\partial_{n+1}(1\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}}x,z,y)\otimes 1)

\begin{split}=&(xy^{k_{1}}+y^{k_{1}+1})\otimes(x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}}x,z,y)\otimes 1+(-1)^{n+1}\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}}x,z)\otimes y\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}(-1)^{i}\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{i-1}}x,y^{k_{i}+k_{i+1}+1}x,y^{k_{i+2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}}x,z,y)\otimes 1.\end{split}

We first compute the comparison morphisms between
P
and
B(A,A)
via Theorem
7.1
and Proposition
7.2

Proposition 7.4
.

The comparison morphism
F:P\to B(A,A)
from the two-sided Anick resolution to the reduced two-sided bar resolution of
A
, is defined as follows:

(i)

In degrees
-1
and
0
,
F
is the identity map;

(ii)

in degree
1
,
F_{1}(1\otimes a\otimes 1)=1\otimes a\otimes 1,

a=x,y,z;

(iii)

in degree
\geqslant 2,

F_{2}(1\otimes(x,z)\otimes 1)=1\otimes x\otimes z\otimes 1,\quad F_{2}(1\otimes(z,y)\otimes 1)=1\otimes z\otimes y\otimes 1,\quad F_{3}(1\otimes(x,z,y)\otimes 1)=1\otimes x\otimes z\otimes y\otimes 1,

and

\hskip-227.62204ptF_{n+1}(1\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x)\otimes 1)

=1\otimes(x\otimes y^{k_{1}}x\otimes y^{k_{2}}x\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n}}x)\otimes 1+\sum_{i=1}^{k_{1}-1}(xy^{i-1}+y^{i})\otimes(y\otimes y^{k_{1}-i}x\otimes y^{k_{2}}x\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n}}x)\otimes 1,

\hskip-227.62204ptF_{n+2}(1\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z)\otimes 1)

=1\otimes(x\otimes y^{k_{1}}x\otimes y^{k_{2}}x\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n}}x\otimes z)\otimes 1+\sum_{i=1}^{k_{1}-1}(xy^{i-1}+y^{i})\otimes(y\otimes y^{k_{1}-i}x\otimes y^{k_{2}}x\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n}}x\otimes z)\otimes 1,

\hskip-227.62204ptF_{n+3}(1\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z,y)\otimes 1)

=1\otimes(x\otimes y^{k_{1}}x\otimes y^{k_{2}}x\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n}}x\otimes z\otimes y)\otimes 1+\sum_{i=1}^{k_{1}-1}(xy^{i-1}+y^{i})\otimes(y\otimes y^{k_{1}-i}x\otimes y^{k_{2}}x\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n}}x\otimes z\otimes y)\otimes 1,

for
n\geqslant 1,
and
k_{1},\cdots,k_{n}\geqslant 0
.

Proof.

∎

The computation of the comparison morphism from the reduced two-sided bar resolution to the two-sided Anick resolution of
A
is more involved.

Proposition 7.5
.

The comparison morphism
G:B(A,A)\to P
from the reduced two-sided bar resolution to the two-sided Anick resolution of
A
, is defined as follows:

(i)

In degrees
-1
and
0
,
G
is the identity map
;

(ii)

in degree
1
, we have
G_{1}(1\otimes a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{n}\otimes 1)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{1,i-1}\otimes a_{i}\otimes a_{i+1,n},\quad a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}\in\{x,y,z\};

(iii)

in degree
\geqslant 2,
we have

G_{2}(1\otimes ax\otimes zb\otimes 1)=a\otimes(x,z)\otimes b;

G_{2}(1\otimes a^{\prime}z\otimes yb^{\prime}\otimes 1)=a^{\prime}\otimes(z,y)\otimes b^{\prime};

G_{2}(1\otimes y^{k_{0}}xy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xb^{\prime\prime}\otimes 1)=y^{k_{0}}\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x)\otimes b^{\prime\prime};

G_{2}(1\otimes a^{\prime}zxy^{m}\otimes y^{m^{\prime}}xb^{\prime\prime}\otimes 1)=-a^{\prime}\otimes(z,y)\otimes y^{m+m^{\prime}}xb+a^{\prime}z\otimes(x,y^{m+m^{\prime}}x)\otimes b^{\prime\prime};

G_{3}(1\otimes ax\otimes z\otimes yb^{\prime}\otimes 1)=a\otimes(x,z,y)\otimes b^{\prime};

G_{3}(1\otimes ax\otimes zxy^{m}\otimes y^{m^{\prime}}xb^{\prime\prime}\otimes 1)=-a\otimes(x,z,y)\otimes y^{m+m^{\prime}}xb^{\prime\prime};

G_{n+1}(1\otimes axy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n}-j_{n}}xb^{\prime\prime}\otimes 1)=a\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x)\otimes b^{\prime\prime};

G_{n+1}(1\otimes axy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}x\otimes zb\otimes 1)=a\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z)\otimes b;

G_{n+2}(1\otimes axy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}x\otimes z\otimes yb^{\prime}\otimes 1)=a\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z,y)\otimes b^{\prime};

G_{n+2}(1\otimes axy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}x\otimes zxy^{m}\otimes y^{m^{\prime}}xb^{\prime\prime}\otimes 1)

\hskip 28.45274pt=-a\otimes(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z,y)\otimes y^{m+m^{\prime}}xb^{\prime\prime},

where
n\geqslant 2,m,m^{\prime},k_{0},k_{1},\cdots,k_{n},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\geqslant 0,
and
ax,a^{\prime}z,zb,yb^{\prime},xb^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{+};

(iv)

the action of
G
is zero on the remaining direct summands of the reduced two-sided bar resolution of
A
.

Proof.

We first observe that in the quiver
\overline{Q_{B}}^{{\mathcal{M}}}
, a thick arrow
(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\to(v_{1},\cdots,v_{n-1})
satisfies that the product
v_{1}\cdots v_{n-1}
is a subword of
w_{1}\cdots w_{n},

or that
w_{1}\cdots w_{n}
reduces to
v_{1}\cdots v_{n-1}
in
A.

Moreover, a dotted arrow
(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\dashrightarrow(v_{1},\cdots,v_{n+1})
in
\overline{Q_{B}}^{{\mathcal{M}}}
satisfies
w_{1}\cdots w_{n}=v_{1}\cdots v_{n+1}.

Consequently, a zigzag path originating from
(w_{1},\dots,w_{n})
to
(v_{1},\dots,v_{m})
in
\overline{Q_{B}}^{{\mathcal{M}}}
satisfies the product
w_{1}\cdots w_{n}
reduces to
av_{1}\cdots v_{m}b
in finitely many steps in
A
for
a,b\in\mathcal{B}.

Next, we assume that there exists a zigzag path in
\overline{Q_{B}}^{{\mathcal{M}}}
originating from
w=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})
to an Anick chain
v=(v_{1},\cdots,v_{n}).
For different
v,

we determine the form of
w
based on previous observations.

(i)

Suppose
n\geqslant 2,
and let
v=(v_{1},\cdots,v_{n})=(x,y^{k_{1}}x,

\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x),
with
k_{1},\cdots,k_{n-1}\geqslant 0.

A direct computation shows that only
axy^{p_{1}}x\cdots y^{p_{m}}xb
(with
m\geqslant n-1,p_{1},\cdots,p_{m}\geqslant 0,
and
ax,xb\in\mathcal{B}
) can reduces to
a^{\prime}v_{1}\cdots v_{n}b^{\prime}(a^{\prime}x,xb^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B})
in finitely many steps. Therefore, the product

w_{1}\cdots w_{n}=axy^{p_{1}}x\cdots y^{p_{m}}xb.

If
m\geqslant n,
then one of the
w_{1},\cdots,w_{n}
must contain
xy^{k_{i}}x
as subword, for some
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1,
which contradicts the condition
w_{1},\cdots,w_{n}\in\mathcal{B}_{+}
.
Hence,
m=n,
and the product
w_{1}\cdots w_{n}=axy^{k_{1}}x\cdots y^{k_{n-1}}xb
for some
a,b\in\mathcal{B}
such that
ax,xb\in\mathcal{B}.

Consequently, the only possibility for
(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})
is of the following form:

w=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})=(axy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},y^{k_{2}-j_{2}}xy^{j_{3}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb),

for
0\leqslant j_{1}\leqslant k_{1},\cdots,0\leqslant j_{n-1}\leqslant k_{n-1}.

The subsequent discussion follows a similar pattern to the cases above. We omit the details and leave it as an exercise.

(ii)

Suppose
n\geqslant 3,
and let
v=(v_{1},\cdots,v_{n})=(x,y^{k_{1}}x,

\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}}x,z),
with
k_{1},\cdots,k_{n-2}\geqslant 0.
Then

w=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})=(axy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},y^{k_{2}-j_{2}}xy^{j_{3}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}-j_{n-2}}x,zb),

for some
a,b\in\mathcal{B}
such that
ax,zb\in\mathcal{B},
and
0\leqslant j_{1}\leqslant k_{1},\cdots,0\leqslant j_{n-2}\leqslant k_{n-2}.

(iii)

Suppose
n\geqslant 4,
and let
v=(v_{1},\cdots,v_{n})=(x,y^{k_{1}}x,

\cdots,y^{k_{n-3}}x,z,y),
with
k_{1},\cdots,k_{n-3}\geqslant 0.
Then either

w=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})=(axy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},y^{k_{2}-j_{2}}xy^{j_{3}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}-j_{n-2}}x,z,yb)

or

w=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})=(axy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},y^{k_{2}-j_{2}}xy^{j_{3}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-2}-j_{n-2}}x,zxy^{l},y^{l^{\prime}}xb^{\prime}),

for some
a,b,b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
such that
ax,yb,xb^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B},l,l^{\prime}\geqslant 0,
and
0\leqslant j_{1}\leqslant k_{1},\cdots,0\leqslant j_{n-3}\leqslant k_{n-3}.

(iv)

Let
v=(x,z,y).
Then either

w=(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3})=(ax,z,yb)\quad\text{or}\quad w=(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3})=(ax,zxy^{l},y^{l^{\prime}}xb^{\prime}),

for some
a,b,b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
such that
ax,yb,xb^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B},
and
l,l^{\prime}\geqslant 0.

(v)

Let
v=(x,z).
Then

w=(w_{1},w_{2})=(ax,zb),

for some
a,b,\in\mathcal{B}
such that
ax,zb\in\mathcal{B}.

(iv)

Let
v=(z,y).
Then either

w=(w_{1},w_{2})=(az,yb)\quad\text{or}\quad w=(w_{1},w_{2})=(azxy^{l},y^{l^{\prime}}xb^{\prime}),

for some
a,b,b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
such that
az,yb,xb^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B},
and
l,l^{\prime}\geqslant 0.

(iiv)

Let
v\in\{x,y,z\}.
Then

w=avb,

for some
a,b\in{\mathcal{B}}
such that
avb\in{\mathcal{B}}.

(iiiv)

If
v=\ast_{0},
then
w=\ast_{0}
; if
v=\ast_{-1},
then
w=\ast_{-1}.

Finally, through computation, we obtain all the even-length zigzag paths from all the aforementioned
w
to an Anick chain, as shown below.

(i)

w=(axy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)
with
n\geqslant 3,

0\leqslant j_{1}\leqslant k_{1},\cdots,0\leqslant j_{n-1}\leqslant k_{n-1},

and
a,b\in\mathcal{B}
such that
ax,xb\in\mathcal{B}.

There is a unique zigzag path from
w
to an Anick chain in
\overline{Q_{B}}^{{\mathcal{M}}},
which is shown as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(axy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a_{1},a_{2}\cdots a_{m}xy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a_{2}\cdots a_{m}xy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a_{m},xy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(xy^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,y^{j_{1}},y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,y^{k_{1}}xy^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,y^{k_{1}}x,y^{j_{2}},\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,y^{k_{1}}x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}-j_{n-1}}xb)}

Next, we will compute the comparison map between the two-sided Anick resolution and the Koszul resolution of
A
via algebraic Morse theory.

Consider the following direct sum decomposition of the two-sided Anick resolution
P
(see Proposition
7.3
) of
A
:

P_{n}\cong\bigoplus_{\omega\in W^{(n-1)}}A\otimes\mathbf{k}\omega\otimes A,n\geqslant 1,\quad P_{0}=A\otimes A,\quad\text{and}\quad P_{-1}=A,

which induces a weighted quiver
Q_{P}
under the notation established at the beginning of this section.
Specifically, with a slight abuse of notation, the direct summands

A\otimes\mathbf{k}\omega\otimes A(n\geqslant 1,\omega\in W^{(n-1)}),\quad P_{0}=A\otimes A,\quad\text{and}\quad P_{-1}=A

correspond to the vertices
\omega,\ast_{0},\ast_{-1}
in
Q_{P}
, respectively.
The arrows in the weighted quiver
Q_{P}
are listed below. For each arrow, its notation is placed to the left, and its weight is indicated above it.

(i)

\partial_{0}:\ast_{0}\xrightarrow{1}\ast_{-1}
;

(ii)

\partial_{1}:a\xrightarrow{a\otimes 1-1\otimes a}\ast_{0},~a\in\{x,y,z\}
;

(iii)

\partial_{2}^{0}:(x,z)\xrightarrow{x\otimes 1}z,

\partial_{2}^{2}:(x,z)\xrightarrow{1\otimes z}x,

\partial_{2}^{0}:(z,y)\xrightarrow{z\otimes 1}y,

\partial_{2}^{2}:(z,y)\xrightarrow{1\otimes y}z,

\partial_{2}^{0,1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x)\xrightarrow{(xy^{k_{1}}+y^{k_{1}+1})\otimes 1+1\otimes y^{k_{1}}x}x,

\partial_{2}^{1,2}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x)\xrightarrow{\sum_{i=1}^{k_{1}}(xy^{i-1}+y^{i})\otimes y^{k_{1}-i}+1\otimes y^{k_{1}}x}y,
with
k_{1}\geqslant 0
;

(iv)

\partial_{3}^{0}:(x,z,y)\xrightarrow{x\otimes 1}(z,y),

\partial_{3}^{3}:(x,z,y)\xrightarrow{-1\otimes y}(x,z)
;

(v)

\partial_{n+1}^{0,1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x)\xrightarrow{(xy^{k_{1}}+y^{k_{1}+1})\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x),

\partial_{n+1}^{n+1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x)\xrightarrow{(-1)^{n+1}\otimes y^{k_{n}}x}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x),

\partial_{n+1}^{i+1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x)\xrightarrow{(-1)^{i+2}\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{i-1}}x,y^{k_{i}+k_{i+1}+1}x,y^{k_{i+2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x),

with
n\geqslant 2,k_{1},\cdots,k_{n}\geqslant 0,1\leqslant n
;

(vi)

\partial_{n+2}^{0,1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z)\xrightarrow{(xy^{k_{1}}+y^{k_{1}+1})\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z),

\partial_{n+2}^{n+2}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z)\xrightarrow{(-1)^{n+2}\otimes z}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x),

\partial_{n+2}^{i+1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z)\xrightarrow{(-1)^{i+2}\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{i-1}}x,y^{k_{i}+k_{i+1}+1}x,y^{k_{i+2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z),

with
n\geqslant 1,k_{1},\cdots,k_{n}\geqslant 0,1\leqslant i\leqslant n
;

(vii)

\partial_{n+3}^{0,1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z,y)\xrightarrow{(xy^{k_{1}}+y^{k_{1}+1})\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z,y),

\partial_{n+3}^{n+3}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z,y)\xrightarrow{(-1)^{n+3}\otimes y}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z),

\partial_{n+3}^{i+1}:(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n}}x,z,y)\xrightarrow{(-1)^{i+2}\otimes 1}(x,y^{k_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{i-1}}x,y^{k_{i}+k_{i+1}+1}x,y^{k_{i+2}}x,\cdots,y^{k_{n-1}}x,z,y),

with
n\geqslant 1,k_{1},\cdots,k_{n}\geqslant 0,1\leqslant i\leqslant n.

In
[
7
, Proposition 7.4]
, a Morse matching for
Q_{P}
was constructed whose corresponding Morse complex is precisely the Koszul resolution of
A
.

Proposition 7.6

(
[
7
]
)
.

The full subquiver
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
of
Q_{P}
defined by the arrows listed below, forms a Morse matching for
Q_{P}
:

(i)

\partial_{n+1}^{2}:(x,x,y^{\ell_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{\ell_{n}}x)\xrightarrow{-1\otimes 1}(x,y^{\ell_{1}+1}x,\cdots,y^{\ell_{n}}x),
with
n\geqslant 1,\ell_{1},\cdots,\ell_{n}\geqslant 0;

(ii)

\partial_{n+2}^{2}:(x,x,y^{\ell_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{\ell_{n}}x,z)\xrightarrow{-1\otimes 1}(x,y^{\ell_{1}+1}x,\cdots,y^{\ell_{n}}x,z)
, with
n\geqslant 1,\ell_{1},\cdots,\ell_{n}\geqslant 0;

(iii)

\partial_{n+3}^{2}:(x,x,y^{\ell_{1}}x,\cdots,y^{\ell_{n}}x,z,y)\xrightarrow{-1\otimes 1}(x,y^{\ell_{1}+1}x,\cdots,y^{\ell_{n}}x,z,y),
with
n\geqslant 1,\ell_{1},\cdots,\ell_{n}\geqslant 0.

Furthermore, the set of critical vertices with respect to
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
is given as follows:

\mathcal{V}^{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}=\{\ast_{-1},\ast_{0},x,y,z,(x,x),(x,z),(z,y),(x,x,z),(x,z,y),(x,x,z,y)\}.

Consequently, the Morse complex
P^{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
induced by
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
is shown below.

0\to A\otimes U_{4}\otimes A\xrightarrow{b_{4}}A\otimes U_{3}\otimes A\xrightarrow{b_{3}}A\otimes U_{2}\otimes A\xrightarrow{b_{2}}A\otimes U_{1}\otimes A\xrightarrow{b_{1}}A\otimes A\xrightarrow{b_{0}}A\to 0,

where

U_{4}=\mathbf{k}\{(x,x,z,y)\},U_{3}=\mathbf{k}\{(x,x,z),(x,z,y)\},U_{2}=\mathbf{k}\{(x,x),(x,z),(z,y)\},U_{1}=\mathbf{k}\{x,y,z\},

and the differential is defined by the following equations:

\begin{array}[]{l}b_{4}(1\otimes(x,x,z,y)\otimes 1)=(x+y)\otimes(x,z,y)\otimes 1+1\otimes(x,x,z)\otimes y,\\
b_{3}(1\otimes(x,x,z)\otimes 1)=(x+y)\otimes(x,z)\otimes 1-1\otimes(x,x)\otimes z,\\
b_{3}(1\otimes(x,z,y)\otimes 1)=x\otimes(z,y)\otimes 1-1\otimes(x,z)\otimes y,\\
b_{2}(1\otimes(x,x)\otimes 1)=(x+y)\otimes x\otimes 1+1\otimes x\otimes x+1\otimes y\otimes x,\\
b_{2}(1\otimes(x,z)\otimes 1)=x\otimes z\otimes 1+1\otimes x\otimes z,\\
b_{2}(1\otimes(z,y)\otimes 1)=z\otimes y\otimes 1+1\otimes z\otimes y,\\
b_{1}(1\otimes a\otimes 1)=a\otimes 1-1\otimes a,a=x,y,z,\\
b_{0}(1\otimes 1)=1.\end{array}

Indeed, the Morse complex
P^{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to the Koszul resolution
K
of
A
(see Proposition
2.1
) under the following one-to-one correspondence.

\displaystyle U_{4}\cong V_{4},

\displaystyle(x,x,z,y)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(x+y)xzy,

\displaystyle U_{3}\cong V_{3},

\displaystyle(x,x,z)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(x+y)xz,

\displaystyle(x,z,y)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow xzy,

\displaystyle U_{2}\cong V_{2},

\displaystyle(x,x)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(x+y)x,

\displaystyle(x,z)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow xz,

\displaystyle(z,y)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow zy,

\displaystyle U_{i}=V_{i},

\displaystyle i=-1,0,1.

Next, we proceed to compute the comparison morphisms between the two-sided Anick resolution and the Koszul resolution of
A
via algebraic Morse theory.

Proposition 7.7
.

The comparison morphism
F^{\prime}:K\to P
from the Koszul resolution to the two-sided Anick resolution of
A
is the canonical inclusion induced by the isomorphism described above.

Proof.

Through direct computation, it can be concluded that any even-length zigzag path originating from the critical vertices associated with
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
must be a trivial path.
Consequently,
F^{\prime}
is canonical embedding according to Theorem
7.1
.

∎

Finally, the comparison morphisms between the two-sided bar resolution and the Koszul resolution of
A
are given by the compositions
F\circ F^{\prime}
and
G^{\prime}\circ G
.

Theorem 7.9
.

The comparison morphisms

\textstyle{\iota:K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B(A,A):\pi\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(18)

between the two-sided reduced bar resolution and the Koszul resolution of
A
are given by the following formulas.

(i)

In degrees
-1,

0
and
1,

\iota
is the identity map
;

(ii)

in degree
2,
we have

\iota_{2}(1\otimes(x+y)x\otimes 1)=1\otimes(x+y)\otimes x\otimes 1,

\iota_{2}(1\otimes xz\otimes 1)=1\otimes x\otimes z\otimes 1,

\iota_{2}(1\otimes zy\otimes 1)=1\otimes z\otimes y\otimes 1;

(iii)

in degree
3,
we have

\iota_{3}(1\otimes(x+y)xz\otimes 1)=1\otimes(x+y)\otimes x\otimes z\otimes 1,

\iota_{3}(1\otimes xzy\otimes 1)=1\otimes x\otimes z\otimes y\otimes 1;

(iv)

in degree
4,
we have

\iota_{4}(1\otimes(x+y)xzy\otimes 1)=1\otimes(x+y)\otimes x\otimes z\otimes y\otimes 1,

and

(v)

in degrees
-1
and
0,

\pi
is the identity map
;

(vi)

in degree
1
, we have

\pi_{1}(1\otimes a_{1}\cdots a_{n}\otimes 1)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{1,i-1}\otimes a_{i}\otimes a_{i+1,n},
with
a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}\in\{x,y,z\};

(vii)

in degree
2
, we have

\pi_{2}(1\otimes ax\otimes zb\otimes 1)=a\otimes xz\otimes b,

\pi_{2}(1\otimes a^{\prime}z\otimes yb^{\prime}\otimes 1)=a^{\prime}\otimes zy\otimes b^{\prime},

\pi_{2}(1\otimes y^{k_{0}}xy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xb^{\prime\prime}\otimes 1)=y^{k_{0}}(x+y)^{k_{1}}\otimes(x+y)x\otimes b^{\prime\prime}-\sum_{i=0}^{k_{1}-1}y^{k_{0}}(x+y)^{i}\otimes(x+y)x\otimes y^{k_{1}-i-1}xb^{\prime\prime},

\pi_{2}(1\otimes a^{\prime}zxy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xb^{\prime\prime}\otimes 1)

\hskip 56.9055pt=-a^{\prime}\otimes zy\otimes y^{k_{1}}xb^{\prime\prime}+a^{\prime}zxy^{k_{1}-1}\otimes(x+y)x\otimes b^{\prime\prime}-\sum_{i=0}^{k_{1}-1}a^{\prime}zxy^{i-1}\otimes(x+y)x\otimes y^{k_{1}-i-1}xb^{\prime\prime},

with
k_{0}\geqslant 0,0\leqslant j_{1}\leqslant k_{1},
and
a,a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}
such that
ax,a^{\prime}z,zb,yb^{\prime},xb^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}};

(viii)

in degree
3
, we have

\pi_{3}(1\otimes axy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}x\otimes zb\otimes 1)=a(x+y)^{k_{1}}\otimes(x+y)xz\otimes b,

\pi_{3}(1\otimes ax\otimes z\otimes yb^{\prime}\otimes 1)=a\otimes xzy\otimes b^{\prime},

\pi_{3}(1\otimes ax\otimes zxy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}xb^{\prime\prime}\otimes 1)=-a\otimes xzy\otimes y^{k_{1}}xb^{\prime\prime},

with
0\leqslant j_{1}\leqslant k_{1},
and
a,b,b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}
such that
ax,zb,yb^{\prime},xb^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}};

(ix)

in degree
4
, we have

\pi_{4}(1\otimes axy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}x\otimes z\otimes yb\otimes 1)=a(x+y)^{k_{1}}\otimes(x+y)xzy\otimes b,

\pi_{4}(1\otimes axy^{j_{1}}\otimes y^{k_{1}-j_{1}}x\otimes zxy^{j_{2}}\otimes y^{k_{2}-j_{2}}xb^{\prime}\otimes 1)=a(x+y)^{k_{1}}\otimes(x+y)xzy\otimes y^{k_{2}}xb^{\prime},

with
0\leqslant j_{1}\leqslant k_{1},0\leqslant j_{2}\leqslant k_{2},
and
a,b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}
such that
ax,yb,xb^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}};

(x)

on all remaining direct summands of
B(A,A)
, the morphism
\pi
vanishes.

8
Connes’ Differential

By the results in Section
3
,
\HH_{n}(A)=0
for
n\geqslant 2
. Since the Connes’ differential is of degree
+1
, the only nontrivial Connes’ differential is
b_{0}:\HH_{0}(A)\to\HH_{1}(A)
.

Proposition 8.1
.

The Connes’ differential
b_{0}:\HH_{0}(A)\to\HH_{1}(A)
at degree
0
is given by

(i)

b_{0}(\alpha_{0})=\alpha_{1},

(ii)

b_{0}(\beta_{0}(n_{0}))=-(n_{0}+1)\cdot\gamma_{1}(n_{0}-1),

(iii)

b_{0}(\beta_{0}(1))=-2\beta_{1},

(iv)

b_{0}(\gamma_{0}(n_{0}))=n_{0}\cdot\epsilon_{1}(n_{0}-1),

(v)

b_{0}(\gamma_{0}(1))=\delta_{1},

(vi)

b_{0}(\delta_{0}(m_{1}))=m_{1}\cdot\eta_{1}(m_{1}-1),

(vii)

b_{0}(\delta_{0}(1))=\zeta_{1},

(viii)

b_{0}(\bar{\epsilon}_{0}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{p};m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}))=\theta_{1}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{p};m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}),

(ix)

b_{0}(\zeta_{0})=0,

where
n_{0},m_{1}\geqslant 2
and
n_{1},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p}\geqslant 1
.

Proof.

Recall from Definition
A.14
that the Connes’ differential is defined on the bar resolution. Then we have
b_{0}=p_{1}\circ\bar{b}_{0}\circ i_{0}
, where
\bar{b}_{0}:\gamma_{0}(A)\to\gamma_{1}(A)
is the map
b_{n}
for
n=0
as in Definition
A.14
and

p,i
are the chain maps between the Hochschild chain complex
A\otimes_{A^{e}}B_{\bullet}(A,A)
and the complex from the Koszul resolution
A\otimes_{A^{e}}K

induced by the comparison maps
\pi
and
\iota
constructed in Theorem
7.9
.
More specifically, the maps
i_{0}
and
p_{1}
are defined by

i_{0}=\id_{A}\quad\text{and}\quad p_{1}(a\otimes a_{1}\cdots a_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i+1,n}aa_{1,i-1}\otimes a_{i},

for
a\in A
,
a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}\in\{x,y,z\}
.
Then we have

b_{0}(A_{0})=p_{1}\circ\bar{b}_{0}\circ i_{0}(x)=p_{1}(1\otimes x)=1\otimes x=A_{1}.

Similarly,

\begin{array}[]{rl}b_{0}(\beta_{n}(n_{0}))&=p_{1}(1\otimes y^{n_{0}}x)=y^{n_{0}}\otimes x+(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y^{n_{0}-i}xy^{i-1})\otimes y\\
&=\left(-(n_{0}+1)y^{n_{0}-1}x\right)\otimes x+d_{2}\left(\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n_{0}-2}y^{j}xy^{n_{0}-j-2}-y^{n_{0}-1}+xy^{n_{0}-2}\right)\otimes(x^{2}+yx)\right)\end{array}

for
n_{0}\geqslant 2
; hence
b_{0}(\beta_{n}(n_{0}))
is homologous to
-(n_{0}+1)y^{n_{0}-1}x\otimes x=-(n_{0}+1)\gamma_{1}(n_{0}-1)
.

For
n_{0}=1,2
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}b_{0}(\beta_{0}(2))=&p_{1}(1\otimes y^{2}x)=y^{2}\otimes x+(yx+xy)\otimes y\\
&=-3yx\otimes x+d_{2}\left((x-y)\otimes(x^{2}+yx)\right),\\
b_{0}(\beta_{0}(1))=&p_{1}(1\otimes yx)=y\otimes x+x\otimes y\\
&=-2x\otimes x-d_{2}(1)\end{array}

These give the images of
\beta_{0}
. Similarly,

\begin{array}[]{c}b_{0}(\gamma_{0}(n_{0}))=p_{1}(1\otimes y^{n_{0}})=n_{0}y^{n_{0}-1}\otimes y=n_{0}\epsilon_{1}(n_{0}-1)\quad(n_{0}\geqslant 2)\\
b_{0}(\gamma_{0}(1))=p_{1}(1\otimes y)=1\otimes y=\delta_{1}\\
b_{0}(\delta_{0}(n_{1}))=p_{1}(1\otimes z^{n_{1}})=n_{1}z^{n_{1}-1}\otimes z\quad(n_{1}\geqslant 2)\\
b_{0}(\delta_{0}(1))=p_{1}(1\otimes z)=1\otimes z=\zeta_{1}\\
b_{0}(\zeta_{0})=p_{1}(1\otimes 1)=0\\
\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{rl}b_{0}(\overline{E}_{0}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p})&=p_{1}(1\otimes y^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}x\cdots y^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}}x)\\
&=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{t=1}^{n_{i}}y^{n_{i}-t}z^{m_{i}}x\cdots y^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}x\cdots xy^{t-1}\right)\otimes y\\
&+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{s=1}^{m_{i}}z^{m_{i}-s}x\cdots y^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}x\cdots xy^{n_{i}}z^{s-1}\right)\otimes z\\
&+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}y^{n_{i+1}}z^{m_{i+1}}x\cdots y^{n_{p}}z^{m_{p}}xy^{n_{1}}z^{m_{1}}x\cdots y^{n_{i}}z^{m_{i}}\right)\otimes x\end{array}

which is exactly
\theta_{1}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{p},m_{1},\cdots,m_{p})
in Proposition
3.4
.
∎

9
Gerstenhaber Bracket

In this section, we will compute the Gerstenhaber bracket on the Hochschild cohomology of
A
.

Although the Gerstenhaber bracket is defined in terms of the two-sided reduced bar resolution (see Definition
A.8
), we will, for computational convenience, transfer its definition to the Koszul cochains via the comparison morphisms
\pi
and
\iota
established in Section
7
.
Specifically, given
f,g\in\Hom_{A^{e}}(K,A),
their Gerstenhaber bracket is defined by:

[f,g]:=\iota^{*}[\pi^{*}(f),\pi^{*}(g)],

where
\iota^{*}
and
\pi^{*}
are the pre-composition maps induced by
\iota
and
\pi,
respectively.
Based on the graded-anticommutativity of the Gerstenhaber bracket and the fact that the Hochschild cohomology of
A
is concentrated in degrees
0,-1,-2,
and
-4,
it suffices to compute the bracket restricted to the following components:

(i)

\HH^{-1}(A)\otimes\HH^{0}(A)\to\HH^{0}(A);

(ii)

\HH^{-2}(A)\otimes\HH^{0}(A)\to\HH^{-1}(A);

(iii)

\HH^{-1}(A)\otimes\HH^{-1}(A)\to\HH^{-1}(A);

(iv)

\HH^{-2}(A)\otimes\HH^{-1}(A)\to\HH^{-2}(A);

(v)

\HH^{-4}(A)\otimes\HH^{-1}(A)\to\HH^{-4}(A).

Furthermore, since
\HH^{0}(A)=\mathbf{k},
the Gerstenhaber bracket vanishes on the components (i) and (ii) mentioned above.

We now proceed to compute the Gerstenhaber bracket on the degree -1 Hochschild cohomology:

[-,-]:\HH^{-1}(A)\otimes\HH^{-1}(A)\to\HH^{-1}(A).

Let
f,g\in\HH^{-1}(A)
be the cochains defined by

f:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}1\otimes x\otimes 1&\mapsto&a\\
1\otimes y\otimes 1&\mapsto&b\\
1\otimes z\otimes 1&\mapsto&c\end{array}\right.\quad\text{and}\quad g:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}1\otimes x\otimes 1&\mapsto&u\\
1\otimes y\otimes 1&\mapsto&v\\
1\otimes z\otimes 1&\mapsto&w,\end{array}\right.

the Gerstenhaber bracket
[f,g]\in\HH^{-1}(A)
is given by:

\begin{array}[]{rl}[f,g](1\otimes x\otimes 1)&=\big([\pi^{*}(f),\pi^{*}(g)]-[\pi^{*}(g),\pi^{*}(f)]\big)\big(\iota(1\otimes x\otimes 1)\big)\\
&=\pi^{*}(f)(1\otimes g(1\otimes x\otimes 1)\otimes 1)-\pi^{*}(g)(1\otimes g(1\otimes x\otimes 1)\otimes 1)\\
&=f(\pi(1\otimes u\otimes 1))-g(\pi(1\otimes a\otimes 1)),\end{array}

and similarly,

[f,g](1\otimes y\otimes 1)=f(\pi(1\otimes v\otimes 1))-g(\pi(1\otimes b\otimes 1)),

[f,g](1\otimes z\otimes 1)=f(\pi(1\otimes w\otimes 1))-g(\pi(1\otimes c\otimes 1)).

Proposition 9.1
.

The Gerstenhaber bracket on the basis elements of
\HH^{-1}(A)
(from Proposition
4.2
) satisfies graded-anticommutativity. Consequently, it suffices to compute the following brackets to determine its complete structure.

Convention.
In the following equations, we set
F^{-1}(n_{0},\cdots,n_{p};m_{1},\cdots,m_{p})=0
if any
n_{i}\leq 0
or
m_{j}\leq 0
for
i,j=1,\cdots,p
, and likewise for
G^{-1},H^{-1},I^{-1},J^{-1},K^{-1}.

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},A^{-1}]=0,

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},B^{-1}(n)]=nB^{-1}(n),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},C^{-1}(n+1)]=(n+1)C^{-1}(n+1),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},D^{-1}(n,i)]=nD^{-1}(n,i),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},E^{-1}(n)]=nE^{-1}(n),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=(\sum\limits_{i=0}^{p}n_{i}+p)F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}n_{i}+p-1)G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=(\sum\limits_{i=0}^{p+1}n_{i}+p)H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p+1}n_{i}+p-1)I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p+1}n_{i}+p)J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[A^{-1},K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=(\sum\limits_{i=2}^{p}n_{i}+p-1)K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),B^{-1}(m)]=(m-n)B^{-1}(m+n),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),C^{-1}(m+1)]=\sum\limits_{i=n}^{n+m-1}D(n+m+1,i),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),D^{-1}(m,i)]=(i+1)D^{-1}(n+m,n+i)+\sum\limits_{j=n+i+1}^{n+m-2}D^{-1}(n+m,j),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),E^{-1}(m)]=\sum\limits_{i=n}^{n+m-2}D^{-1}(m+n,i),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=(n_{0}+1)F^{-1}(n_{0}+n,n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}F^{-1}(n_{0}+n+i,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-2}F^{-1}(n+i,n_{1}-i-1,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=(n_{0}+1)H^{-1}(n_{0}+n,n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+n+i,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-2}H^{-1}(n+i,n_{1}-i-1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),J^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n+i,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[B^{-1}(n),K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=0,

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),C^{-1}(m+1)]=0,

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),D^{-1}(m,i)]=0,

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),E^{-1}(m)]=-nC^{-1}(n+m+1)+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D^{-1}(n+m+1,i),

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}F^{-1}(n+n_{0}+i+1,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}}F^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1}+n-i+1,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{l=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m_{l}-1}F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,l},n,n_{l+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,l-1},i,m_{l}-i,m_{l+1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},n-i+1;m_{1,\cdots,p})-F^{-1}(n_{0}+n+1,n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-2}J^{-1}(n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},n-i;m_{1,\cdots,p})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-2}F^{-1}(n+i+1,n_{1}-i-1,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}F^{-1}(i,n+n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{l=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m_{l}-1}G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,l},n,n_{l+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,l-1},i,m_{l}-i,m_{l+1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+n+i+1,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}}H^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1}+n-i+1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{l=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m_{l}-1}H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,l},n,n_{l+1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,l-1},i,m_{l}-i,m_{l+1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}H^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-i+1;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-2}J^{-1}(n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-i;m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-2}H^{-1}(n+i+1,n_{1}-i-1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(i,n+n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{l=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m_{l}-1}I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,l},n,n_{l+1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,l-1},i,m_{l}-i,m_{l+1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n+i+1,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}}H^{-1}(i,n_{1}+n-i+1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}H^{-1}(i,n_{1,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-i+1;m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{l=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m_{l}-1}J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,l},n,n_{l+1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,l-1},i,m_{l}-i,m_{l+1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[C^{-1}(n+1),K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]

\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum\limits_{l=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m_{l}-1}K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,l},n,n_{l+1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,l-1},i,m_{l}-i,m_{l+1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[D^{-1}(n,i),D^{-1}(m,j)]=0,

\diamondsuit

[D^{-1}(n,i),E^{-1}(m)]=\sum\limits_{j=i+1}^{n-1}D^{-1}(n+m,j)-(n-i-1)D^{-1}(n+m,i),

\diamondsuit

[D^{-1}(n,i),F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n_{1}-1}F^{-1}(n_{0}+n+j,n_{1}-j,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n_{1}}F^{-1}(n_{0}+i+j,n+n_{1}-i-j,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})-\sum\limits_{j=i+1}^{n-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+j,n_{1,\cdots,p},n-j;m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-F^{-1}(n_{0}+n,n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[D^{-1}(n,i),G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n_{1}-2}F^{-1}(n+j,n_{1}-j-1,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{j=i}^{n_{1}+i-1}F^{-1}(j,n+n_{1}-j-1,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-i-2}H^{-1}(i,n_{1}+j,n_{2,\cdots,p},n-i-j-1;m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+F^{-1}(i,n+n_{1}-i-1,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[D^{-1}(n,i),H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+n+j,n_{1}-j-1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n_{1}}H^{-1}(n_{0}+i+j,n_{1}+n-i-j,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{j=i+1}^{n}H^{-1}(n_{0}+j,n_{1,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-j;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[D^{-1}(n,i),I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n_{1}-2}H^{-1}(n+j,n_{1}-j-1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{j=i}^{n_{1}+i-1}H^{-1}(j,n+n_{1}-j-1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-i-1}H^{-1}(i,n_{1}+j,n_{2,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-i-j-1;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[D^{-1}(n,i),J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n+j,n_{1}-j-1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n_{1}}H^{-1}(i+j,n_{1}+n-i-j,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})-\sum\limits_{j=i+1}^{n}H^{-1}(j,n_{1,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-j;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[D^{-1}(n,i),K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=0,

\diamondsuit

[E^{-1}(n),E^{-1}(m)]=-\sum\limits_{i=n}^{m+n-2}D^{-1}(m+n,i)+\sum\limits_{i=m}^{m+n-2}D^{-1}(m+n,i)+(m-n)E^{-1}(m+n),

\diamondsuit

[E^{-1}(n),F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-1}F^{-1}(n_{0}+n+i,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},n-i;m_{1,\cdots,p})+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}n_{i}F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,i-1},n_{i}+n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[E^{-1}(n),G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-2}F^{-1}(n+i,n_{1}-i-1,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}n_{i}G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},n_{i}+n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})-G^{-1}(n_{1}+n,n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}I^{-1}(n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},n-i;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[E^{-1}(n),H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+n+i,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}n_{i}H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,i-1},n_{i}+n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})+n_{p+1}H^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n;m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-i;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[E^{-1}(n),I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-2}H^{-1}(n+i,n_{1}-i-1,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}n_{i}I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},n_{i}+n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})+n_{p+1}I^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n;m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n}I^{-1}(n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-i;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[E^{-1}(n),J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n+i,n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}n_{i}J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,i-1},n_{i}+n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p})+(n_{p+1}-1)J^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n;m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n-1}H^{-1}(i,n_{1,\cdots,p},n_{p+1}+n-i;m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[E^{-1}(n),K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p})]=\sum\limits_{i=2}^{p}n_{i}K^{-1}(n_{2,\cdots,i-1},n_{i}+n,n_{i+1,\cdots,p+1};m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),F^{-1}(r_{0,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q})]

\hskip 17.07164pt=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r_{1}-1}F^{-1}(r_{0}+n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},r_{1}-i,r_{2,\cdots,q};m_{1,\cdots,p},s_{1,\cdots,q})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}F^{-1}(r_{0}+n_{0}+i,r_{1,\cdots,q},n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p};s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),G^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q})]

\hskip 17.07164pt=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{r_{1}-2}F^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},r_{1}-i-1,r_{2,\cdots,q};m_{1,\cdots,p},s_{1,\cdots,q})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{j}-2}F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,j-1},r_{1}+i,r_{2,\cdots,q},n_{j}-i-1,n_{j+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,j-1},s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{j,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),H^{-1}(r_{0,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})]

\hskip 17.07164pt=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r_{1}-1}H^{-1}(r_{0}+n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},r_{1}-i,r_{2,\cdots,q+1};m_{1,\cdots,p},s_{1,\cdots,q})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}}F^{-1}(n_{0}+r_{0}+i,r_{1,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}+n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p};s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),I^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})]

\hskip 17.07164pt=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{r_{1}-2}H^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},r_{1}-i-1,r_{2,\cdots,q+1};m_{1,\cdots,p},s_{1,\cdots,q})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{j}-1}F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,j-1},r_{1}+i,r_{2,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}+n_{j}-i-1,n_{j+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,j-1},s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{j,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),J^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})]

\hskip 17.07164pt=-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r_{1}-1}H^{-1}(n_{0}+i,n_{1,\cdots,p},r_{1}-i,r_{2,\cdots,q+1};m_{1,\cdots,p},s_{1,\cdots,q})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n_{1}}F^{-1}(n_{0}+i,r_{1,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}+n_{1}-i,n_{2,\cdots,p};s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m_{j}-1}F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,j},r_{1,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}-1,n_{j+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,j-1},i,s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{j}-i,m_{j+1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),K^{-1}(r_{2,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q})]

\hskip 17.07164pt=-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m_{j}-1}F^{-1}(n_{0,\cdots,j},r_{2,\cdots,q},n_{j+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,j-1},s_{1}+i,s_{2,\cdots,q-1},s_{q}+m_{j}-i-1,m_{j+1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),G^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q})]

\hskip 17.07164pt=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{q}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{r_{j}-2}G^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,j-1},n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},r_{j}-i-1,r_{j+1,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,j-1},m_{1,\cdots,p},s_{j,\cdots,q})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{j}-2}G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,j-1},r_{1}+i,r_{2,\cdots,q},n_{j}-i-1,n_{j+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,j-1},s_{1\cdots,q},m_{j,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

[G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,p}),H^{-1}(r_{0,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,q})]

\hskip 17.07164pt=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{q}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{r_{j}-2}H^{-1}(r_{0,\cdots,j-1},n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},r_{j}-i-1,r_{j+1,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,j-1},m_{1,\cdots,p},s_{j,\cdots,q})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{r_{q+1}-2}H^{-1}(r_{0,\cdots,q},n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},r_{q+1}-i-1;s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{1}-1}F^{-1}(r_{0}+i,r_{1,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}+n_{1}-i-1,n_{2,\cdots,p};s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{1,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit

\hskip 17.07164pt=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{q}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{r_{j}-2}I^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,j-1},n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},r_{j}-i-1,r_{j+1,\cdots,q+1};s_{1,\cdots,j-1},m_{1,\cdots,p},s_{j,\cdots,q})

\vskip 2.84526pt\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{r_{q+1}-2}I^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q},n_{1}+i,n_{2,\cdots,p},r_{q+1}-i-1;s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt+G^{-1}(r_{1,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}+n_{1}-1,n_{2,\cdots,q};s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{1,\cdots,p})

\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n_{j}-1}G^{-1}(n_{1,\cdots,j-1},r_{1}+i,r_{2,\cdots,q},r_{q+1}+n_{j}-i-1,n_{j+1,\cdots,p};m_{1,\cdots,j-1},s_{1,\cdots,q},m_{j,\cdots,p}),

\diamondsuit
